## P6-14 — Transition Priority Selection + Ambiguity Detection (6.14 / 6.15) — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-03)
1. SCOPE: WBS 6.14 (L653-678) + 6.15 (L682-697), TDD T6.14.1–T6.14.4 / T6.15.1–T6.15.4 — a PURE, deterministic priority-based transition selection over a set of persisted transitions, plus ambiguity detection. Priority policy: LOWER numeric value = HIGHER priority (matches the WBS 6.14 example 10 > 20 > 30), documented on the `PriorityPolicy` const and shared by both functions. Selection: matching = same source state + enabled + outcome match under the existing `MatchOutcome` case policy (T6.9.4); highest-priority (lowest numeric) match selected; nonmatching/disabled skipped; equal-priority tie at the minimum rejected as ambiguity. Detection: two enabled transitions sharing (source state, normalized outcome, priority) are ambiguous — rejected and reported with the offending state + transition IDs. No tie-break beyond priority is needed because the ambiguity rejection makes the min-priority match unique; ordering is a pure function of persisted data so it is stable across restart. Pure domain in `internal/state` (arch-guard clean); integration tests drive them with real persisted rows via the dbrepo transition repository.

## P6-15 — Graph Checks: Reachability / Terminal-State / Zero-Progress Cycles (6.16 / 6.17 / 6.18) — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-03)
1. SCOPE: WBS 6.16 (L699-723) + 6.17 (L726-748) + 6.18 (L751-778), TDD T6.16.1–T6.16.4 / T6.17.1–T6.17.3 / T6.18.1–T6.18.4 — three PURE graph checks over a machine's persisted states + transitions in the arch-guard-clean `state` package: (a) reachability from the initial state over ENABLED transitions, orphan detection, initial state reachable by definition; (b) terminal-state validation — terminal with outgoing enabled transition rejected, terminal without outgoing valid, nonterminal dead-end detected; (c) zero-progress cycle detection — pure-control cycles rejected, execution loops and condition-guarded continuation loops allowed, involved states reported. Structured reports (offender state lists) so the P6-16 aggregate validator can surface them; deterministic ordering (sorted by key, then ID). Two policies recorded in ACTIVE_TASK.md notes + doc comments: DISABLED-ORPHAN POLICY — disabled transitions excluded from the reachability graph, so an enabled state reachable only through a disabled edge is flagged, and disabled states are inert/never reported; CYCLE CLASSIFICATION RULE — a cycle is zero-progress iff every state in it is Kind CONTROL and none of its transitions carries a condition (HasCondition); a non-CONTROL kind marks intentional worker execution, a condition marks a context/progress guard.
2. ## P6-17 — State Machine Versioning (6.20) — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-03)
1. SCOPE: WBS 6.20 (L816-842), TDD T6.20.1–T6.20.5 — state-machine versioning: edits create new versions rather than silently altering the workflow governing active/historical execution. Design: a machine family = the set of state_machines rows sharing one stable family_id (the v1 row's id). An edit appends a NEW machine row (family_id inherited, version = advisory-locked MAX+1 under a bounded retry on the new UNIQUE (family_id, version) constraint) plus fresh workflow_states/state_transitions rows carrying the new version number, so v1 and every intermediate version remain retrievable with its states/transitions reconstructable, and an active execution's (machine, version) binding is never mutated. Migration 00029 (no number shift; 00028 was the prior max): version columns (NOT NULL DEFAULT 1) on workflow_states/state_transitions; workflow_states unique key moved from (machine_id, key) to (machine_id, version, key); family_id added to state_machines (add → backfill → NOT NULL, since Postgres forbids a column reference in DEFAULT) + UNIQUE (family_id, version). Service layer: StateMachineVersioningService.Edit (input validation → locked MAX+1 → new rows); no REST surface in WBS 6.20 (lands P6-25) — the op is exercised by in-package integration tests over the live harness pool.
2. ## P7-22 — Materialization API — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-05)

### Scope (WBS 7.39)
Project-scoped materialization REST surface over the P7-14/P7-15/P7-16 StateFileMaterializeService (no parallel drift computation path — the constraint in ACTIVE_TASK.md):
- POST /api/v1/projects/{project}/state-files/materialize — writes the authoritative persisted state-file content into the project's working directory (<working-dir>/.overseer/state/<DefaultFilename>); response identifies each materialized file (id, path, version, content hash) plus per-file skip reasons.
- GET /api/v1/projects/{project}/state-files/drift — per-file drift status (SYNCHRONIZED/DRIFTED/MISSING/NOT_MATERIALIZED) against the persisted authoritative versions, via the P7-16 DetectDrift.
- No arbitrary server filesystem paths: the destination is the project's CONFIGURED working directory (server-resolved from project settings via SettingsService.GetSettings); a client-supplied path target in the POST body is rejected (the strict decode accepts NO fields — T7.39.3); a path-like query parameter is ignored; writes stay inside the project directory by construction (the service only writes under <working-dir>/.overseer/state/ — T7.39.4).
- Unknown/malformed project path segment → 404 (established project-scoped contract); malformed project id is a web-layer NewNotFoundError, NOT a 400.
- Stable web-layer DTOs (stateFileMaterializedEntry / stateFileDriftEntry), strict body decode on the POST, the standard serviceError mapping (400/404/409/500) + the web-layer not-found passthrough (matServiceError).
- Wiring in app.Start per the established REST task pattern (the metadata repository wired, per the P7-15 pattern).

### Test IDs
T7.39.1 Materialization succeeds; T7.39.2 Drift status returned; T7.39.3 Arbitrary destination rejected; T7.39.4 Project working-directory isolation enforced.

## P7-23 — Context-Preview API + Snapshot Read API — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-05)

### Scope (WBS 7.37 + 7.40)
Two read-only REST surfaces over the existing snapshot pipeline (no new migration, no parallel assembly path — the ACTIVE_TASK.md constraint):
- GET /api/v1/projects/{project}/context-preview — reports WHAT context WOULD be captured for the project's current runtime position: the current state key, the resolved role key, the role's required state files each at its CURRENT persisted version (version + content hash + token estimate), the required files that are MISSING (no file at the resolved scope), and the total estimated context token count. Non-persisting (no snapshot row, no history) and non-mutating (runtime row untouched — T7.37.4/T7.37.5).
- GET /api/v1/projects/{project}/context-snapshots — list the project's persisted historical snapshots (oldest first; empty array, never null, when none).
- GET /api/v1/projects/{project}/context-snapshots/{snapshot} — retrieve one persisted snapshot by id (its position fields, resolved role, pinned file versions + hashes + budget); unknown (or cross-project) id → 404. Read-only — no mutation endpoint (T7.40.4).

The preview reuses the P7-18 creation pipeline (role resolution + P7-13 scope resolution + P7-19/20 version/hash + token estimate) — the distinguishing property over the creation op is that a missing required FILE is REPORTED (MissingFiles, present=false, version 0) instead of failing with ErrMissingStateFile (T7.37.3).

### Test IDs
T7.37.1 requirements displayed; T7.37.2 versions displayed; T7.37.3 missing files identified; T7.37.4 preview doesn't persist a snapshot; T7.37.5 preview doesn't alter runtime; T7.40.1 snapshot retrieved; T7.40.2 historical versions represented; T7.40.3 unknown snapshot 404; T7.40.4 mutation endpoint unavailable.

The strict P7-13 op fails on the FIRST missing type, so the preview re-resolves per type to report the COMPLETE missing set.
- The estimated token count includes the resolved role's system-prompt + instruction content (the same components the P7-18 snapshot budget uses), in addition to the present required files — an estimate of the whole context, not just the files.
- GetSnapshot enforces project scope in the service (a snapshot of a different project is ErrNotFound under this project's URL — the established project-scoped read contract), not just row existence.
- The snapshot list returns an empty (non-nil) array for a project with none (stable JSON representation, not null).

# TASK P8-23 — Response Schema Composition

## Objective
Compose the per-state response schemas (WBS 8.41/8.42/8.43): planning (unit fields/dependencies/acceptance/tests/kinds from configuration), implementation (status/allowed/summary/remaining — no next-unit selection), and validation (PASS/FAIL/criteria/tests/failures) response schemas as prompt components, built on the P8-22 response-protocol composition. Phase 8 includes schema/instructions only — parsing/acceptance occurs later.

## References
- WBS 8.41 Planning Response Schema Composition (Phase 8 Prompt Template.md L1358-1393), tests T8.41.1-T8.41.5.
- WBS 8.42 Implementation Response Schema Composition (Phase 8 Prompt Template.md L1395-1420), tests T8.42.1-T8.42.5.
- WBS 8.43 Validation Response Schema Composition (Phase 8 Prompt Template.md L1422-1445), tests T8.43.1-T8.43.5.
- P8-22 composition (internal/prompt/responseprotocol.go) and P8-09 protocol model as the base.

## Dependencies
- P8-22 (DONE)

## Required Changes
- Pure-domain response-schema composition in internal/prompt: compose the planning, implementation, and validation response schemas as prompt components — planning specifies the required unit fields (id/kind/objective/dependencies/acceptance/tests/constraints) with work-unit kinds from configuration; implementation specifies status/allowed statuses/summary/remaining without instructing the worker to select the next WBS unit; validation supports PASS/FAIL, criteria results, test results, and failures.
- TDD: tests in internal/prompt written FIRST from WBS 8.41/8.42/8.43 test IDs, RED confirmed, then implementation.

## Acceptance Criteria
- [x] T8.41.1 — Required unit fields specified.

- [x] T8.41.4 — Tests specified.
- [x] T8.41.5 — Work-unit kinds specified from configuration.
- [x] T8.42.1 — Status required.
- [x] T8.42.2 — Allowed statuses specified.
- [x] T8.42.3 — Summary required according to protocol.
- [x] T8.42.4 — Remaining work supported.
- [x] T8.42.5 — Worker isn't instructed to select next WBS unit.

## Constraints

- T8.41.5: work-unit kinds come from a configuration input (caller-supplied kinds), not a hard-coded list — mirror the P8-22 captured-values discipline (no "latest" input).

## P8-31 — Canonical Prompt Hash — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-06)

1. SCOPE (WBS 8.27, Phase 8 Prompt Template L943-963): pure-domain canonical prompt hash in internal/prompt — deterministic over the final canonical prompt + semantic metadata; same inputs → same hash, each input change → new hash. No REST endpoint, no service op, no new migration. Hash-input set (stated per task NOTES so TESTER/PM can verify): (1) canonical content (byte-for-byte; a continuation-status change rides here because the continuation component is part of the render — T8.27.5), (2) template version (T8.27.3), (3) snapshot hash (the context snapshot's content hash, standing in for the state files it pins — T8.27.2), (4) response protocol version, (5) role version (a role-instruction change advances it — T8.27.4), (6) state-machine version + state key (the pinned identity of the composed state). Serialization is per-field length-prefixed (8-byte BE length over tag+payload) so field boundaries are unambiguous and no input can cancel another's contribution; the digest is a single SHA-256 over the frame — the same primitive as P8-02's ContentHash, so both share one hash domain.

## P8-36 — Prompt Cache-Key Preparation — PM ACCEPTANCE (2026-10-06)

1. Scope (WBS 8.34 L1156-1182, Phase 8 Prompt Template.md): pure-domain cache-key preparation — a deterministic cache-input structure over the canonical prompt's pinned inputs exposing the inputs Phase 10 will use for cache keys. Chosen cache-input field set (stated in test comments + source, verifiable by TESTER/PM): project configuration version; state-machine version + state key; role configuration hash (WBS 5.16 ConfigurationHash); template version + hash; execution (context) snapshot hash; response protocol version + hash; continuation status hash (empty string = no continuation captured — a fresh prompt pins ""). Change-detection policy: every field is a CAPTURED/pinned value — the serialization never reads "latest" state; the cache input is the hex SHA-256 of a per-field length-prefixed serialization (the same framing primitive as P8-31's CanonicalPromptHash, so both share one hash domain), and a change to ANY single field moves the cache input. - Verify the WBS 8.47 checklist is present: ENGINEER identity, state instructions, project state, architecture, phase state, active task, acceptance criteria, required tests, captured status, remaining work, response protocol.

## PHASE 9 — Prompt Compression (PLAN + completion record, archived 2026-10-07)

# PLAN — Phase 9: Prompt Compression Service & Semantic Preservation

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 9 Prompt Compression.md (WBS §9.1–9.60 + DoD L1850-1925). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited from Phase 8 (COMPLETE; P8-49 DoD gate, PM acceptance 2026-10-07): prompt components + CompressionPolicy markers + DefaultPolicy (internal/prompt/component.go), canonical prompt domain/persistence/hash (00044), token estimator (bytes/4), size verdicts (sizevalidation.go), cache-key framing (frameField), versioned delimiter + escape (delimit.go), sensitive-data registry, composer service (service/p838_composer.go), history/provenance APIs. Project settings already carry compression_provider/compression_model/context_window/context_threshold (00003).
Phase 8 gaps Phase 9 must close (contract extensions, APPROVED by user 2026-10-07; record in ACTIVE_TASK notes): (a) canonical prompt components + policies are NOT persisted (P8-49 note) — compression needs them; (b) acceptance criteria/tests/constraints carry no stable IDs in the canonical render — IDs (AC-n/TEST-n/CONSTRAINT-n) are assigned in a persisted preservation manifest instead (canonical content + hash stay byte-identical) [user: recommended approach]; (c) response format is PRESERVE_EXACT for compression — RESPONSE_PROTOCOL raised to exact (raise-only, Phase 8 DefaultPolicy untouched) [user: exact].
User requirement (2026-10-07, beyond WBS): any content wrapped in `<preserve></preserve>` tags, in any component regardless of its policy, is never compressed, paraphrased or dropped — treated as PRESERVE_EXACT (task P9-10A, tests U9.P.1–U9.P.7).
New in Phase 9: compression domain (internal/compression), provider-independent Compressor + SemanticValidator interfaces, compression settings + provider registry, preservation manifest, compression instruction versioning, structured policy-framed input, deterministic validators + pipeline, attempt/retry/backoff/fallback/bypass policy, compressed/worker prompt artifacts + hashes, OpenAI-compatible HTTP adapter (cloud + local), PrepareWorkerPrompt service, preview/history/metrics/diff APIs, integration + acceptance tests, DoD gate. Phase 9 does NOT dispatch the development worker.
Test PG: postgres://pguser:***@172.17.0.1:5432/devpipe?sslmode=disable (override OVERSEER_TEST_PG_DSN).

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; archtest guard green for every task. internal/compression is a pure domain package (no internal/database, internal/web, internal/gateway, internal/compressionprovider, net/http). Provider code lives only in internal/compressionprovider (new, non-domain); no provider SDK dependency — stdlib net/http + encoding/json.
- No Hermes Gateway / development-worker call anywhere in Phase 9. Tests never call a real LLM: unit tests use the scripted mock compressor; integration/e2e use an httptest OpenAI-compatible fake.
- Canonical prompt row is never updated; all compression artifacts are separate append-only tables. Failed compressed output is never a retry source — every attempt is built from the canonical components + manifest.
- Migration numbering (verify `ls migrations/` max+1 at execution; record shifts in ACTIVE_TASK notes): 00046 canonical_prompt_components; 00047 canonical_prompt_preservation_items; 00048 project_compression_settings; 00049 compression_instructions; 00050 compression_runs; 00051 compression_attempts; 00052 compressed_prompts; 00053 worker_prompts. Every migration task bumps schema_migrations version pins in prior integration tests (grep the integration tree) and records each bump as an accepted contract change.
- Repositories: domain interface in internal/persistence (no pgx), PostgreSQL implementation in internal/dbrepo; TranslateDBError at the boundary; WithTx returns the interface type; supplied timestamps written explicitly.
- Retry budget: one MaxAttempts budget covers validation failures and transient provider failures; backoff only before a retry following a retryable provider error; permanent errors stop immediately → fallback.
- Preservation IDs (AC-n/TEST-n/CONSTRAINT-n) are overseer-assigned in the manifest by captured index, never parsed from model output; canonical content/hash unchanged by Phase 9.
- Credentials: read from the env var named in app config at call time; never persisted, logged, rendered in prompts/previews/provenance/errors (redaction tested with a sentinel key).
- Token counts: prompt estimator (bytes/4) is authoritative for policy decisions; provider-reported usage is recorded metadata only.
- Preview persists nothing; history/metrics/diff endpoints are read-only (405 via mux on write methods).
- `<preserve>` regions: the inner text must appear verbatim in compressed output (tags themselves may be kept or dropped); regions count toward exact tokens for ErrCompressionTargetImpossible; the default compression instruction (P9-17) states the rule; untrusted content can only make content MORE preserved, never less.

## Implementation notes (2026-10-07, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- P9-19 framing: sections are delimited by a deterministic boundary token (`CB<16 hex>`, derived from a content hash + counter until absent from every section) instead of the delimit.go backslash escape — escaping would alter exact text the model must copy verbatim. Unspoofable (token never occurs in content) and byte-reconstructable (ParseStructuredInput).
- P9-23 config hash also covers the context window (the NEVER rejection + canonical-fallback decisions depend on it; found by TestP949ForcedModes — a shrunk window must not reuse an earlier result).
- P9-10 EffectivePolicy also raises CONTINUATION_CONTEXT to PRESERVE_EXACT (WBS 9.51: completed/remaining/blocking must never be swapped).
- Composer (Phase 8 contract extensions, content/hash unchanged): WithComponents (components + manifest in the canonical tx); PHASE_PLAN WBS document + planning configuration components carry PRESERVE_EXACT (WBS 9.49); ComposePromptRequest.CapturedStatus optionally carries captured status lists (WBS 9.51).
- Extra endpoints beyond WBS: POST .../prompts/{prompt}/worker-prompt (PrepareWorkerPrompt), GET .../worker-prompts/{worker}/lineage (WBS 9.34/9.59 queryable edges). Metrics: GET .../compression-metrics. Diff: GET .../compression-attempts/{attempt}/diff (attempt UUID).
- Persistence: runs are created PENDING and finalized once; attempts, compressed prompts and worker prompts are immutable (UPDATE rejected by trigger). Down-migration test P7-05 drops the Phase 9 tables first (accepted contract change). schema_migrations pins bumped 45 → 53 in 16 integration tests (accepted contract change).
- Packages: internal/compression (domain), internal/compression/compressiontest (scripted + heuristic doubles), internal/compressionprovider (+ providertest fake server), internal/service/compression*.go, internal/web/compression.go, internal/dbrepo/compression.go, internal/persistence/compression_repository.go, migrations 00046-00053.

## PHASE 10 — Prompt Cache (PLAN + completion record, archived 2026-10-07)

# PLAN — Phase 10: Prompt Cache, Invalidation & Reuse

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 10 Prompt Cache.md (WBS §10.1–10.70 + DoD L2071-2151). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited from Phase 9 (COMPLETE 2026-10-07; record in TASK_ARCHIVE.md): compression domain + CompressionService.PrepareWorkerPrompt(canonicalID) (Layer 2 = compression_runs.request_identity reuse), immutable compressed_prompts / worker_prompts, ConfigHash (provider/model/instruction/target/context window/mode/policy/validation), SingleFlight, composer WithComponents + CapturedStatus. Phase 7/5 supply ExecutionContextSnapshot.SnapshotHash() (derived, not persisted; covers project/phase/unit/runtime version/machine version/state key/role ConfigurationHash/state-file versions+hashes) and RoleSnapshot.ConfigurationHash() (system prompt, ordered instructions, allowed states, state-file requirements). Phase 8 supplies template/protocol content_hash columns and prompt.CacheKey (P8-36, unversioned input set — superseded by the Phase 10 versioned key, kept for compatibility).
Composition inputs NOT pinned by the snapshot (read live by the P8-38 composer) must enter cache identity explicitly: work-unit specification (objective/acceptance/tests/constraints/dependencies), state instruction version+hash, phase WBS version+hash (PHASE_PLAN), planning policy/granularity (project config), captured continuation status. P10-07 extracts one shared source resolver so the composer and the cache key read the exact same values (no drift).
New in Phase 10: versioned semantic cache key + layered cache (L1 canonical / L2 compression / L3 worker prompt), prompt_cache_entries (VALID/BUILDING/FAILED/INVALIDATED, lease, negative window, warm-state source, dependency classification, key-source metadata), cache events (audit + metrics + activity read model), context-level PrepareWorkerPrompt (lookup before composition), integrity scan, retention/GC, prompt fragments (static prefixes), REST (prepare/inspect/explain/invalidate/stats/activity), integration + acceptance tests, DoD gate. Phase 10 does NOT precompute future states (Phase 11).
Test PG: postgres://pguser:***@172.17.0.1:5432/devpipe?sslmode=disable (override OVERSEER_TEST_PG_DSN).

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; archtest guard green for every task. internal/cache is a pure domain package (already in the guard's domain list): no database/web/gateway/compressionprovider/net/http imports.
- PostgreSQL is authoritative; any in-memory structure (single-flight) is acceleration only — correctness never depends on RAM (restart tests).
- Cache entries reference immutable Phase 8/9 artifacts (canonical/compressed/worker prompts); they never store prompt strings. Lookup is by (project_id, cache_key) — never by comparing prompt content.
- Key = versioned hash of semantic inputs only, in a fixed framed order (no map iteration, sorted lists); project id participates in lookup isolation; display names, timestamps and secrets never enter keys or key metadata.
- Natural invalidation by key change; historical entries are never deleted by invalidation (only GC deletes eligible cache-entry rows, never artifacts).
- Cache hits never mutate WBS/work-unit/runtime state.
- Migration numbering (verify `ls migrations/` max+1 at execution): 00054 prompt_cache_entries; 00055 canonical_prompts.cache_key; 00056 prompt_cache_events; 00057 prompt_fragments; 00058 project_prompt_cache_settings. Each migration task bumps the schema_migrations pins (`version != NN`) in internal/integration and records the bump as an accepted contract change; P7-05 down-migration drop list extended when a new table references canonical_prompts/worker_prompts.
- Repositories: interface in internal/persistence (no pgx), implementation in internal/dbrepo, TranslateDBError at the boundary, WithTx returns the interface.
- Tests never call a real LLM (scripted mock / Phase 9 providertest fake through the real adapter).

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. Snapshot hash includes the runtime-state version (WBS 10.7 lists it). Consequence: a new snapshot after any workflow transition misses even when prompt content is identical; reuse is within a runtime version. Phase 11 precomputation will need either the expected future runtime version or a cache identity without it — revisit at Phase 11 planning.
2. Uniqueness is a partial unique index over (project_id, cache_key, layer) for VALID/BUILDING rows (WBS 10.30 "UNIQUE(project_id, cache_key)") so INVALIDATED/FAILED history can coexist with a later valid entry for the same key (WBS 10.33.3).
3. Cache identity adds inputs the WBS 10.2 struct omits but the composer reads live (work-unit spec, state instruction, phase WBS); without them T10.21.x / T10.20.2 cannot hold.
4. Context window changes miss in both directions (T10.25.3 "configured identity policy" = strict), inherited from the Phase 9 config hash.
5. Prompt preparation by context becomes the primary entry point (POST .../worker-prompts/prepare); the P8-38 composer is wired into the app for it.

## Implementation notes (2026-10-07, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- Layer 1 key (CanonicalKey) = composition inputs only (snapshot, machine/state, role, template, protocol, work-unit spec, state instruction, phase WBS + planning config for PHASE_PLAN only, continuation); Layer 3 key adds project config (worker provider/model/context), compression config hash and preparation policy. Planning policy/WBS enter only planning prompts (T10.24.3).
- Template / protocol / state-instruction hashes are VersionedHash(kind, id, version, content): a new version misses even with identical text (WBS 10.8).
- Found by TestP1035: after an entry is invalidated, the rebuild must not re-serve the revoked worker prompt through Phase 9 Layer 2 reuse — CompressionService.PrepareWorkerPromptExcluding(skip) + PromptCacheRepository.WorkerPromptRevoked.
- Collision protection compares the stored KeySourceMetadata with the current input on every hit; mismatch → quarantined (INVALIDATED, reason CORRUPT) + CACHE_CORRUPT, never dispatched.
- Event types beyond WBS 10.66: CACHE_BYPASS (policy / cache-layer error) and CACHE_SUPPRESSED (negative window). Stats/activity are derived from events + entries (restart-safe).
- Fragments are recorded (STORE / fragment hit events) on every composition but composition does not yet consume them (Phase 11 precomputation); a fragment has no conversion to a worker prompt.
- Coverage limits stated: T10.21.2 dependency change and T10.23.2 new state-machine version are pinned at the key/domain level (cache tests) — the Phase 8 composer loads no dependency names and the rig cannot advance a running machine version cheaply; state-instruction edits are covered end-to-end.
- Composer wired into the app (P10-27) via the new POST .../worker-prompts/prepare; P8-38 composer split into ResolveComposition + ComposeResolved (P10-07) — composition output unchanged (all P8/P9 tests green).
- Test support: p9Rig template key made unique per call; providertest.Reply.Delay added; harness unchanged.
- Migrations 00054-00058 as planned; schema pins bumped 53 → 58 (16 integration tests); P7-05 down-migration drops prompt_cache_entries first (accepted contract changes).

## PHASE 11 — Future-State Prompt Precomputation (PLAN + completion record, archived 2026-10-07)

# PLAN — Phase 11: Future-State Prompt Precomputation & Cache Warming

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 11 Future State.md (WBS §11.1–11.86 + DoD L2522-2618). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited from Phase 10 (COMPLETE 2026-10-07; record in TASK_ARCHIVE.md): PromptCacheService.PrepareForContext (L1 canonical / L2 compression / L3 worker layers, BUILDING leases, negative window, explain, events, stats, fragments + BuildFragments, KeySourceMetadata, dependency classes), composer ResolveComposition / ComposeResolved, CompressionService. Phase 6 supplies the versioned state-machine graph (states, transitions, enabled flags, state→role assignments, workflow_states.prompt_template key) and TransitionApplicationService; Phase 4/2 supply execution plans, WorkUnitRepository.ListEligible (dependency-ready units) and unit status ops; Phase 7 supplies ExecutionSnapshotService.assemble, scope resolution, ActiveTaskService + statefile.RenderActiveTask; Phase 3 supplies LifecycleService (PAUSED/CANCELLED).
Not yet built (later phases): worker dispatch (Phase 12), worker-response handling (Phase 13), continuation orchestration (Phase 14), autonomous phase loop (Phase 16). Phase 11 therefore exposes trigger hooks those phases call, plus a manual API; tests drive "actual transitions" with the existing Phase 4/6/7 services.
New in Phase 11: cache key schema v2 (runtime-independent snapshot identity), projected execution contexts (persisted projections, never fabricated snapshots), reachable-state discovery, candidate model + dependency registry + readiness classification, next-unit discovery, projection-based preparation (FULLY_KNOWN → PRECOMPUTED worker prompts via the Phase 10 cache), static fragments + fragment manifests + fragment completion (PARTIALLY_KNOWN), async jobs with bounded worker pool + budgets + resource policy + cancellation/pause, candidate records, usage/utilization/latency metrics, audit events, REST (jobs/manual/candidates/read model/metrics), integration + acceptance tests, DoD gate. Phase 11 never calls a development worker, never transitions state, never changes work-unit status.
Test PG: postgres://pguser:***@172.17.0.1:5432/devpipe?sslmode=disable (override OVERSEER_TEST_PG_DSN).

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; archtest guard green (internal/precompute is a pure domain package — add it to the guard's domain list).
- Phase 11 coordinates Phase 7–10 components; it never implements its own composition, compression, validation or cache-key generation (T11.1.2-T11.1.4).
- Hard boundary (WBS 11.35): no development-worker call, no state transition, no work-unit status change, no ACTIVE_TASK / state-file / runtime write, no repository-source change. Tests assert row-level equality of runtime_state / work_units / state_files before and after.
- Unknown worker output, test results, validation failures and generated plans are never fabricated; a candidate needing them is at most PARTIALLY_KNOWN.
- A precomputed prompt is used only through the normal Phase 10 lookup with the authoritative key (WBS 11.23); the only other reuse path is placeholder resolution (P11-26A), which re-validates the substituted prompt in full against the real canonical prompt before storing it under the real key.
- A prompt containing a placeholder marker is never dispatched, cached as a worker prompt, or stored as a VALID entry.
- PostgreSQL authoritative; every job/candidate/projection/manifest/latency record persisted; in-memory state (pool, worker-activity tracker) is acceleration only.
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00059 project_precompute_settings; 00060 precompute_projections; 00061 canonical_prompts.projection_id; 00062 precompute_jobs; 00063 precompute_candidates; 00064 fragment_manifests; 00065 transition_latencies; 00066 precomputed_templates. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration; extend the P7-05 down-migration drop list when new tables reference canonical_prompts / context_snapshots (accepted contract changes).
- Tests never call a real LLM (scripted mock / providertest fake).

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Cache key schema v2 drops the runtime-state id/version from the snapshot contribution** — APPROVED by user 2026-10-07 (option A of: A drop runtime from key / B predict runtime version / C dual keys / D re-key alias / E content-addressed compression reuse only). Phase 10 (Decision 1) included it as WBS 10.7 lists it, but then a key projected before a transition can never equal the actual key after it (the runtime version advances on every transition), making T11.23.1 / T11.62 / T11.65 impossible. Runtime version carries no prompt semantics; every input that does (project, phase, unit, machine version, state, role hash, state-file versions/hashes) stays in the key. Phase 10 v1 entries remain auditable and simply miss. Risk control: a guard test fails if the composition input gains a runtime-derived field that is not a cache-key input.
2. Projections are persisted as their own rows referencing the real base snapshot; canonical prompts built from a projection point at the base snapshot plus projection_id. No historical snapshot is fabricated (T11.20.4).
3. Required dynamic inputs come from a registry keyed by the target state's prompt-template purpose (configurable data), not from what the Phase 8 composer currently renders (it has no worker-result/test-output components yet; Phase 13 adds them).
4. Fragment completion runs the normal full composition with the dynamic input and verifies/records the precomputed fragment; static portions are not compressed separately (whole-prompt validation stays mandatory, WBS 11.48).
5. **ACTIVE_TASK placeholder (user requirement, 2026-10-07).** A future unit projected without an ACTIVE_TASK file gets a placeholder value in the projection instead of a guessed one. The placeholder lives in the TASK_CONTEXT (PRESERVE_EXACT) slot, so it survives compression verbatim. The placeholder-bearing canonical + compressed prompts are stored as a *pending template* (never a worker prompt). When the unit is actually processed: the real ACTIVE_TASK value is calculated (Phase 7 derivation for the now-current unit when the file is missing or stale), the real canonical prompt is composed normally, the placeholder in the precomputed compressed text is replaced with the real value, and the result must pass the full Phase 9 validation pipeline against the real canonical components (exact fragments, manifest, semantics, size) before it is stored under the real cache key. Any mismatch or validation failure → normal preparation. A prompt containing a placeholder can never be dispatched.
6. Worker-activity (for the resource policy) is tracked in-process from the hooks; "local" provider = loopback/private base URL.
7. Precompute audit events reuse the prompt_cache_events log (new PRECOMPUTE_* types).

## Implementation notes (2026-10-07, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- Placeholder pin is version 1, not 0: composition requires a captured state-file version (version 0 surfaced as ErrMissingStateFileVersion → infrastructure failure). The pin hash (sha256 of the token), never the version, distinguishes it from a real ACTIVE_TASK.
- ACTIVE_TASK placeholder trigger: a projected unit lacking an ACTIVE_TASK file is detected through the target role's required state files (ACTIVE_TASK's file type is not itself "required"); without this check the file was silently omitted instead of placeholdered.
- Pending templates are compressed once via CompressionService.CompressTemplate (compression_runs outcome 'TEMPLATE', migration 00066 extends the CHECK) and resolved at use by AdoptPrecomputed (full Phase 9 re-validation; ErrAdoptRejected → normal preparation). Worker-prompt constructors and structural validation reject placeholder markers unless AllowPlaceholders (template path only).
- Base snapshot for discovery = latest snapshot matching the current position, else a newly created one. Candidate preview (GET .../precompute/candidates) never persists: it assembles an in-memory base with a synthetic deterministic (SHA-1) id so projection validation holds.
- Same-session continuation bypass now records fragments (bypass() calls recordFragments), so CONTINUE manifests exist without compression.
- Precompute lookups never Touch an entry or emit LOOKUP/MISS events, never bypass, and a precompute build failure opens no negative window (an on-demand request must not be blocked by a speculative failure).
- Dynamic-input registry defaults: keyed by template key, then purpose (CONTINUE → WORKER_STATUS; VALIDATE → IMPLEMENTATION_RESULT+TEST_RESULTS; FIX → VALIDATION_FAILURES; VALIDATE_PLAN → GENERATED_PLAN).
- Next-phase planning candidates require a WBS for the phase (an appended phase becomes discoverable when its WBS version exists — TestP1133).
- Lifecycle: LifecycleService.WithStatusHook notifies after commit; PAUSED/CANCELLED cancel running jobs. Tests use PrecomputeService.WithBeforeCandidate for deterministic mid-job pause.
- Known limitations (deferred, not fabricated): the Phase 8 composer has no implementation-result / validation-failure sections yet (Phase 13), so FIX/VALIDATE "actual failures inserted" (11.66/11.81) is asserted as "no fabrication + static fragment reuse"; state-machine version race (11.69) and dependency-change (11.68) coverage is at key level.
- Gate evidence: gofmt/vet/build clean; `go test ./... -count=1` and `-race` green; archtest green (precompute in domain list); leak probe 0; live binary (schema 66, precompute endpoints, key/password redaction, graceful shutdown) LIVE_OK.

## PHASE 12 — Hermes Gateway Worker Adapter (PLAN + completion record, archived 2026-10-08)

# PLAN — Phase 12: Hermes Gateway Worker Adapter & Session Control

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 12 Hermes Gateway.md (WBS §12.1–12.103 + DoD L2892-3004). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited (Phase 11 COMPLETE 2026-10-07; record in TASK_ARCHIVE.md): compression.WorkerPrompt (immutable, Content + Hash + EstimatedTokens), PromptCacheService / PrecomputeService (hooks NotifyWorkerDispatched / NotifyWorkerFinished / PrepareNext), project_settings (working_directory, worker_provider, worker_model, context_window, worker_timeout), project.ValidateWorkingDirectory, LifecycleService, config secret-by-env-reference pattern (compression_providers.api_key_env), archtest guard (internal/worker domain; internal/gateway infra), App.Shutdown sequence. Empty packages exist: internal/worker (domain), internal/gateway (adapter), internal/execution, internal/orchestration.
Not built (later phases): response protocol parsing (Phase 13), continuation/context-reset policy (Phase 14), autonomous loop (Phase 16), UI (Phase 17). Phase 12 is transport + session only: it never parses COMPLETE/INCOMPLETE, transitions state, changes work units, runs tests, resets sessions on its own, or resends ambiguous work.
Test PG: postgres://pguser:***@172.17.0.1:5432/devpipe?sslmode=disable (override OVERSEER_TEST_PG_DSN). No live Hermes: all tests use the fake gateway (P12-31) or the mock worker (P12-25).

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; archtest guard green (internal/worker is domain; internal/gateway/hermes is adapter-only, P12-41).
- No Hermes-specific type outside internal/gateway/hermes; services and web speak only `worker.*`.
- Persist intent before the external side effect: dispatch PENDING row and session lease exist before Send; session rows change only after gateway confirmation.
- Never resend: no code path in Phase 12 calls Send twice for one dispatch; reconnection never replays; DELIVERY_UNKNOWN is terminal for Phase 12 (status UNKNOWN).
- Never create a session implicitly (lost session, reconnect, restart, high context).
- Hard boundary: no runtime_state / work_units / state_files / phase writes; tests assert row equality before/after.
- Secrets: auth secret only via env reference; absent from logs, events, DB rows, API bodies, errors.
- Raw responses stored byte-exact and immutable; prompt content never copied into dispatch rows or APIs (prompt id + hash only).
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00067 worker_sessions; 00068 project_worker_settings; 00069 worker_dispatches; 00070 worker_responses; 00071 worker_usage_observations; 00072 worker_session_leases; 00073 worker_events; 00074 worker_tool_events. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration (66 → current max).
- Tests never contact a real Hermes Gateway or LLM.

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Hermes Gateway wire protocol is not specified in the repo.** The adapter defines an adapter-local JSON-over-HTTP contract (P12-27/P12-30): `GET /v1/health` (provider version, protocol version, capabilities), `POST /v1/sessions` (≙ "new"), `GET /v1/sessions/{id}`, `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/cwd` (≙ "cd <dir>"), `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/messages` (headers X-Request-ID, Idempotency-Key; JSON reply with content, usage, metadata), `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/messages/{request}/cancel`, optional SSE stream. Only P12-27/P12-30/P12-31/P12-32 depend on it; if the real gateway differs (e.g. chat commands `/new` and `cd` sent as messages), only those tasks change.
2. Package layout: domain `internal/worker` (+ `internal/worker/workertest` contract suite + mock); adapter `internal/gateway/hermes` (+ `hermestest` fake). Only `internal/app` constructs the adapter.
3. Worker endpoint config lives in app config `worker_providers` (mirrors `compression_providers`); `project_settings.worker_provider` names the entry; Model = worker_model, RequestTimeout = worker_timeout, ContextWindow = context_window. AuthSecretRef = env var name; the value is resolved at connect time and never stored.
4. Delivery detection: the HTTP client records whether the request was fully written (net/http/httptrace WroteRequest). Failure before → DEFINITELY_NOT_SENT; after → DELIVERY_UNKNOWN; response received but malformed/oversized → SENT_RESPONSE_RECEIVED + PROTOCOL_ERROR (dispatch FAILED, UNSAFE_TO_RETRY).
5. Dispatch status mapping: DEFINITELY_NOT_SENT → FAILED (SAFE_TO_RETRY); DELIVERY_UNKNOWN → UNKNOWN (REQUIRES_RECOVERY); pre-send cancellation → CANCELLED; in-flight cancellation → CANCELLED only when the gateway confirms the cancel, else UNKNOWN; provider error replies (auth, rate limit, context limit, invalid) → FAILED with SENT_RESPONSE_RECEIVED.
6. Same-session concurrency: DB session lease (one dispatch per session, also across instances); default concurrent_send = REJECT (409, ErrSessionBusy), optional WAIT (bounded by wait_ms). Lease TTL default 60 s, renewed during the request.
7. Single-active-per-project is enforced by a partial unique index over session rows carrying the policy they were created under, plus the ReplaceActive transaction.
8. Estimated telemetry = WorkerPrompt.EstimatedTokens (+ response length / 4), source ESTIMATED; used only when neither gateway-actual nor provider-derived usage exists.
9. Raw prompt content is never exposed by Phase 12 APIs (there is no authorization model yet); dispatches carry prompt id + hash.
10. Phase 11's WORKER_DISPATCH trigger is fed from DispatchService (P12-69); Phase 12 still never transitions state.

## Implementation notes (2026-10-08, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- Services: one `service.WorkerService` split by concern across files (worker_service.go registry + events, worker_session.go, worker_dispatch.go, worker_recovery.go, worker_read.go) instead of separate Session/Dispatch/Recovery/Read services; the PLAN task boundaries map 1:1 to those files.
- Domain additions beyond WBS 12.1 (the `Worker` interface itself is unchanged): optional `worker.Connector` (Connect / ConnectionState), `worker.Reconciler` (Reconnect / ReconcileSession — CONFIRMED adopts the session as current), `worker.Observable` (transport-change observer feeding audit events), `worker.Settings` (session policy, concurrent-send REJECT/WAIT, lease, wait).
- Dispatch-key header is `X-Overseer-Dispatch-Key`, NOT `Idempotency-Key`: net/http treats requests carrying `Idempotency-Key` as replayable and silently re-sends them after a connection drop — that would duplicate a prompt (WBS 12.89). Recorded in wire.go.
- Delivery classification refinements: an intermediary 502/504 without a gateway error envelope is DELIVERY_UNKNOWN (the upstream may have executed); explicit gateway rejections that prove non-execution (RATE_LIMITED, WORKER_UNAVAILABLE, SESSION_BUSY) classify SAFE_TO_RETRY even though a response was received.
- Dispatch admission refuses (409 `working_directory_not_ready`, nothing persisted or sent) when the worker supports directory control, the project has a configured working directory and the session's confirmed directory is UNKNOWN or different (WBS 12.62 / 12.99 "reconstruction prompt sent only after CWD succeeds"). Workers without the capability are unaffected (WBS 12.97).
- Ordering: the session lease is acquired before the PENDING row (a busy session persists nothing); PENDING → SENDING → WAITING are both written before transmission; restart recovery therefore treats orphaned SENDING/WAITING as UNKNOWN (never resent) and PENDING as FAILED/DEFINITELY_NOT_SENT. Only orphaned dispatches (no live lease naming them) are reclassified, so a second live instance's in-flight dispatch is untouched; a crashed instance's dispatch is reclassified once its lease (default 60 s) expires.
- Dispatch streams when the worker advertises streaming (tool activity persisted to worker_tool_events, assembled response persisted), else uses Send; both transmit exactly once.
- Same-session concurrency is enforced twice: DB lease (cross-instance, REJECT → `ErrSessionBusy`/409, WAIT → bounded poll) and an in-adapter one-in-flight guard (SESSION_BUSY).
- `ConnectionState` transition table also allows DISCONNECTED → RECONNECTING (explicit reconnect after a transport drop). Extra audit/metric event `WORKER_CONNECTION_FAILED` (source of the "connection failures" metric).
- Audit payloads: keys containing content/prompt/response/token/secret/authorization/password/api_key are dropped, except `*_hash` / `*_bytes` (digests and sizes).
- Read models never create or connect a worker (DISCONNECTED until first use or startup recovery). Raw response content is not exposed by the dispatch API (hash + size only); Phase 13 reads it via `WorkerService.Response`. Worker settings have a service API (`UpdateWorkerSettings`) but no REST endpoint (none in WBS).
- Web: new 502 sentinel (`ErrBadGateway`) for upstream worker failures; worker error codes are `worker_<code>` (e.g. `worker_unavailable`, `worker_session_lost`).
- App: `worker_providers` config (yaml + `OVERSEER_WORKER_PROVIDER_*` env) → Hermes provider JSON in app.go; startup recovery bounded at 30 s; shutdown closes workers after precompute and before PostgreSQL (WBS 12.67).
- Fake gateway (hermestest) keeps a request executing for a 500 ms grace period after a client disconnect (a real gateway does not stop on disconnect), so provider cancellation is observable.
- Live-check tooling fix: earlier phases' live scripts ran their schema/table queries against the `devpipe` base database instead of the live-test database (it carries the same migrations, so they passed); live12.py queries the live database.
- Known limitation (PLAN Decision 1, still open): the Hermes Gateway wire protocol is the adapter-local contract in internal/gateway/hermes/wire.go; replacing it with the real gateway API only touches wire.go / client.go / hermestest / testdata.

# PLAN — Phase 13: Worker Response Protocol & Structured Result Parsing

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 13 Worker Response.md (WBS §13.1–13.103 + DoD L2634-2750). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited (Phase 12 COMPLETE 2026-10-08; record in TASK_ARCHIVE.md): worker_dispatches (authoritative project/phase/unit/state/role/session/runtime binding, request_id), worker_responses (raw content byte-exact + content_hash, immutable), `WorkerService.Response`, worker_events audit log, worker usage observations (authoritative context telemetry), Phase 8 response protocols (response_protocols / versions / state_protocol_references; protocol id+version+content hash already enter the L1/L3 cache key), prompt.ContinuationStatus (Phase 8 continuation input), Phase 11 precompute, workertest + hermestest fakes, integration p12 rig.
Phase 13 answers only "what did the worker report?": it never transitions state, marks units DONE, runs tests, changes ACTIVE_TASK / WBS progress / acceptance records, resets sessions or sends worker requests.

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; internal/workerresult is a pure domain package (add to the archtest domain list); it imports no Hermes / gateway / web / database code.
- The parser is deterministic: same raw bytes + same settings + same parser version → same outcome and same normalized hash. No LLM call, no network, no filesystem access.
- Authoritative metadata (project, phase, work unit, session, state, role, request) always comes from the Phase 12 dispatch row; worker-authored values are never applied.
- Parsing reads the stored Phase 12 raw response only; Phase 13 never sends a worker request (tests assert the fake gateway's message count is unchanged by parsing / reparse / recovery).
- Hard boundary: no runtime_state / work_units / state_files / phases / projects / acceptance / validation-record writes; tests assert row equality before/after.
- A protocol parse failure is never a WorkerResult with status failed; nothing is inferred from prose.
- Raw responses stay immutable; parse attempts store hashes and validation details, not raw content; audit events and logs carry classifications, not result text.
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00075 project_result_protocol_settings; 00076 worker_results; 00077 worker_result_items; 00078 worker_result_artifacts; 00079 worker_result_acceptance_claims; 00080 worker_result_parse_attempts; 00081 worker_events result event types; 00082 worker_dispatches.worker_model. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration (74 → current max); extend the P7-05 down-migration drop list if new tables reference dropped tables.
- Tests never contact a real Hermes Gateway or LLM.

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Defaults: parse_mode STRICT, repair_mode DETERMINISTIC_ONLY** (WBS recommendations). Extraction vs repair: STRICT accepts only a body that is exactly one JSON object (surrounding whitespace allowed). TOLERANT additionally accepts exactly one ```json fence spanning the whole body. Unwrapping that fence or stripping a UTF-8 BOM counts as deterministic repair (Repaired=true, method recorded) and is disabled under NONE. LLM_REPAIR is a recognised configuration value but not implemented: readiness reports it as unavailable (WBS 13.34 "if future configuration enables").
2. **Unknown / prohibited fields:** v1 known optional fields are `tests`, `artifacts`, `acceptance_claims`. Any other field: STRICT → SCHEMA_INVALID (UNKNOWN_FIELD; PROHIBITED_FIELD for authoritative names such as project_id / work_unit_id / role / state / context_used); TOLERANT → ignored and never applied. A response declaring a newer minor (1.x, x > supported) is accepted and its unknown fields are ignored (forward-compatible minors); major ≠ 1 → UNSUPPORTED_VERSION.
3. **Per-status rules beyond the WBS minimum:** INCOMPLETE requires a non-empty next_action (the continuation needs it); BLOCKED / COMPLETE / FAILED allow an empty next_action; COMPLETE with an empty `completed` list is accepted (WBS "should normally"); FAILED's explanation is the always-required summary, blocking optional.
4. **Normalization:** CRLF/CR → LF, trim surrounding whitespace of every string, exact-duplicate list items removed keeping first-occurrence order; arrays are ordered (never sorted). The normalized hash covers only worker-reported content + protocol version (not metadata, provenance or timestamps), so a fenced and a raw rendering of the same result hash identically.
5. **Acceptance claims:** ids are matched against the dispatched work unit's acceptance-criterion ids; unknown ids are retained with Recognized=false (never rejected, never applied). Claims never verify criteria.
6. **One accepted result per dispatch** (unique dispatch_id). Re-parse creates a new audited attempt; if an accepted result exists it is never overwritten — the attempt records DUPLICATE (same normalized hash) or a differing outcome for an administrator to review.
7. **Parsing trigger:** synchronous after the Phase 12 dispatch completes (WorkerService hook), plus a startup sweep for COMPLETED dispatches without any parse attempt, plus the manual reparse API. A previously rejected dispatch is only reinterpreted through explicit reparse.
8. **Response-protocol instructions** reuse the Phase 8 response-protocol system: a global IMPLEMENTATION_RESPONSE protocol whose version content is the Phase 13 instruction text. Its id/version/hash already enter the prompt cache key (Phases 8–10), so a protocol change gives a new semantic identity and invalidates precomputed prompts (13.94/13.95) without new cache code.
9. **Worker model for compliance grouping** is recorded on the dispatch at send time (migration 00082) rather than read from current project settings, so grouping uses authoritative dispatch metadata.
10. Limits defaults: result JSON 256 KiB, summary 4 KiB, item 2 KiB, completed 100, remaining 100, blocking 50, artifacts 500, acceptance claims 200.
11. Worker result events reuse the Phase 12 worker_events log (new types), keeping one per-project worker audit stream.

## Implementation notes (2026-10-08, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- **Decision 8 revised (instructions seed):** the global IMPLEMENTATION_RESPONSE slot is already used by existing projects/tests, so there is no automatic startup seed. `ResultService.EnsureProtocol(project)` (REST: POST .../settings/result-protocol/instructions) creates the project-scoped IMPLEMENTATION_RESPONSE protocol `worker-result-protocol` with `workerresult.Instructions(version)` as v1, or appends a new version to the project's existing implementation protocol when its current content differs. State pins are never changed implicitly (Phase 8 pins reference explicit versions). Readiness reports "response protocol instruction not resolvable" until the visible protocol's current content equals the configured instructions. Added `ResponseProtocolLookup.FindByKind` (project scope first, then global) to dbrepo.
- Domain package `internal/workerresult` (registered in the archtest domain list): status, version + compatibility, types, settings + readiness, error model, extraction, a token-level JSON tree (duplicate keys at any depth, depth limit 16), schema + normalization + artifact path safety, semantics, canonical serialization/hash, `DeterministicParser`, payloads (+ bridge to Phase 8 `prompt.ContinuationStatus` bound to the dispatched unit), instructions text, compliance metrics, UI view. Acceptance ids are derived from the unit's criteria prefix ("AC-1: …" → "AC-1").
- Parser input is the stored Phase 12 `worker.WorkerResponseRecord` + `worker.WorkerDispatch` (the WBS 13.8 signature with the persisted response type). A response whose dispatch/project differs is `ErrBindingMismatch` (integrity error, not a protocol outcome); a stored response that no longer matches its content hash is RESULT_MALFORMED / RAW_HASH_MISMATCH.
- Error classes: oversized raw response, invalid UTF-8, BOM without repair, truncated/invalid JSON, trailing content and fence-in-STRICT → RESULT_MALFORMED; empty / prose / fence not spanning the body → RESULT_MISSING; two top-level objects or two fences → RESULT_AMBIGUOUS; field-level type/required/empty/size/unknown/prohibited problems → RESULT_SCHEMA_INVALID; per-status contradictions → RESULT_SEMANTIC_INVALID; major ≠ 1 → RESULT_UNSUPPORTED_VERSION. LLM_REPAIR configured → parsed deterministically; readiness reports LLM_REPAIR unavailable.
- Prohibited authoritative field list (STRICT rejects, TOLERANT ignores, never applied): project_id, phase_id, work_unit_id, dispatch_id, request_id, session_id, state, state_key, role, role_key, runtime_state_id, context_used, context_tokens, context_window, metadata.
- Parsing flow: synchronous completion hook (`WorkerService.WithCompletionObserver`) after a dispatch is COMPLETED, startup sweep `RecoverUnparsed` (COMPLETED dispatches with a stored response and no attempt), explicit `Reparse` (REST). A persistence failure after a successful parse records **no** attempt and returns an error (so recovery/reparse replays it); transient infrastructure errors are retried (bounded, 3) without reparsing differently. Concurrent parsers: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (dispatch_id) DO NOTHING` — exactly one ACCEPTED attempt, the other DUPLICATE, both observe the same result.
- Reparse of a dispatch with an accepted result → DUPLICATE attempt (differs_from_accepted flags a different normalized hash) or REJECTED with differs=true; the accepted result is never overwritten.
- DB guards: worker_results binding trigger requires project/session/request/phase/unit/state/role equal to the dispatch's; results, items, artifacts, claims and attempts are immutable. Migration 00078 originally had a `chr(0)` CHECK on artifact paths, which PostgreSQL rejects at runtime ("null character not permitted"); removed (NUL is rejected by the domain and cannot be stored in TEXT anyway).
- Worker result events reuse worker_events (00081 extends its CHECK); payloads carry classification, field:code lists and hashes only. Dispatches record `worker_model` (00082) and the parse attempt snapshots role/state/provider/model for compliance grouping.
- Result API exposes worker content labelled "worker-reported" (tests, acceptance claims) / "worker-reported, advisory" (next_action), with "Validation: NOT YET RUN" (no deterministic validation records exist before a later phase). Extra read endpoints beyond WBS 13.67/13.68: results/current (13.70 read model), results/metrics (13.62/13.63), results/readiness (13.97); settings GET/PUT (13.96).
- Fuzz: `FuzzP1320Parse` (seeded with the fixture suite) ran ~6.7 M executions in 30 s without a panic or an invalid accepted result.

## PHASE 14 — Context & Continuation State Machine (PLAN + completion record, archived 2026-10-08)

# PLAN — Phase 14: Context & Continuation State Machine

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 14 Context Continuation.md (WBS §14.1–14.130 + DoD L2877-3026). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited (Phase 13 COMPLETE 2026-10-08; record in TASK_ARCHIVE.md): `ResultService` (ParseDispatch / RecoverUnparsed / ResultByDispatch; `WorkerResult.Continuation()` normalized status; one accepted result per dispatch), Phase 12 `WorkerService` (NewSession — confirm-then-persist, previous session RESET; ChangeDirectory — confirmed CWD persisted; Dispatch(DispatchRequest) — PENDING→…→COMPLETED/FAILED/UNKNOWN with delivery state + error code; Reconnect/reconcile → LOST; usage observations per dispatch with UsageSource precedence; `worker.WorkerUsage.Utilization`; `WithCompletionObserver`), Phase 7 `ExecutionSnapshotService` + context_snapshots (runtime state id/version, role snapshot JSON, file pins), Phase 8 canonical composer (`CanonicalCompositionInput.Continuation`, `newContinuationComponent`, CONTINUE_WORK_UNIT template seed, `prompt.ContinuationStatusInput`), Phase 10 `PromptCacheService.PrepareForContext` (PrepareRequest.Continuation + ContinuationMode SAME_SESSION / RESET_SESSION; `cache.ContinuationStatusHash` already in the semantic key; `CacheSameSessionContinuation` setting), Phase 11 precompute (CONTINUE_WORK_UNIT → InputWorkerStatus, template-only), `LifecycleService` (Pause/Resume/Cancel, WithStatusHook), project_settings.context_threshold (00003, CHECK 0 < t ≤ 1, default 0.8), worker_events log, hermestest fake + integration p12/p13 rigs.
Dispatch chain for the source snapshot: worker_dispatches.worker_prompt_id → worker_prompts.canonical_prompt_id → canonical_prompts.context_snapshot_id.
Phase 14 decides and drives continuation of the CURRENT execution only: it never marks work units / phases DONE, advances units or phases, runs tests, writes TEST_STATE, or transitions the Phase 6 runtime state.
Test PG: postgres://pguser:***@172.17.0.1:5432/devpipe?sslmode=disable (override OVERSEER_TEST_PG_DSN).

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; internal/continuation is a pure domain package (archtest domain list); it imports no gateway / web / database code (may import worker / workerresult / prompt domain types).
- Policy is pure and deterministic; threshold logic lives only in `continuation.Policy` (no comparisons elsewhere). Context comes only from Phase 12 usage observations; worker-result text never feeds the decision.
- Every external side effect (NewSession, ChangeDirectory, Dispatch) is preceded by a persisted intent status and followed by a persisted confirmed status; no fixed sleeps as correctness mechanisms.
- Each Advance is a versioned CAS + transition-history row in one transaction; events are emitted only after commit.
- No path ever resends a dispatch whose delivery is unknown or whose row already exists.
- Hard boundary: no work_units / phases / runtime_state / state-file-content / acceptance / validation writes from Phase 14 code; tests assert row equality before/after (new writes: continuation tables, context snapshots, and Phase 12 sessions/dispatches through WorkerService).
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00083 project_continuation_settings; 00084 continuations; 00085 continuation_transitions; 00086 worker_result_consumptions; 00087 worker_dispatches.continuation_id; 00088 worker_events continuation types. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration (82 → current max); extend the P7-05 down-migration drop list if new tables reference dropped tables.
- Tests never contact a real Hermes Gateway or LLM; ordering assertions use the hermestest fake's recorded operation log.

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Threshold source:** the existing project_settings.context_threshold (00003, CHECK 0 < t ≤ 1, default 0.8) is the 14.5 setting; no new threshold column. Other continuation knobs live in project_continuation_settings (00083) with WBS defaults: unknown → fresh_session, estimates may reset, max continuations 8, max resets 4, max no-progress 3, session-create retries 0, lease 60 s.
2. **Lifecycle edges:** PENDING→DECIDING; DECIDING→SAME_SESSION | RESET_REQUIRED | RECOVERY_REQUIRED; SAME_SESSION→RESTORING_CWD | RESET_REQUIRED (session found lost); RESET_REQUIRED→CREATING_SESSION; CREATING_SESSION→RESTORING_CONTEXT | RESET_REQUIRED (retry) | RECOVERY_REQUIRED; RESTORING_CONTEXT→RESTORING_CWD; RESTORING_CWD→READY | RECOVERY_REQUIRED; READY→DISPATCHING | RESTORING_CWD (restart re-check); DISPATCHING→WAITING | RESET_REQUIRED | RECOVERY_REQUIRED; WAITING→COMPLETED | BLOCKED | FAILED | RECOVERY_REQUIRED | RESET_REQUIRED; FAILED / CANCELLED from every nonterminal. Terminal: COMPLETED, BLOCKED, FAILED, CANCELLED, RECOVERY_REQUIRED. A manual retry creates a NEW continuation (retry_of_id) rather than reopening a terminal one. Pause changes no status.
3. **Source binding:** a continuation needs a source result, except triggers CONTEXT_LIMIT / SESSION_LOST on an initial dispatch (14.44), which bind only the source dispatch; captured status then comes from the unit's latest accepted result, or is empty and marked unknown (never invented).
4. **Estimated context:** EstimatedMayReset=true (default) → estimates are compared like telemetry and the decision records Estimated=true. When false, an estimate is treated as unknown (UnknownContextPolicy applies).
5. **Exact threshold:** compare tokens/window ≥ threshold without float drift (e.g. integer comparison at 10⁻⁶ threshold resolution); reason at_threshold when equal, above_threshold when greater.
6. **Session lost / context limit during a continuation dispatch:** the same continuation returns to RESET_REQUIRED (same attempt; reset counted when the new session is confirmed) — transport-level recovery never creates a new attempt (14.36). DELIVERY_UNKNOWN always → RECOVERY_REQUIRED.
7. **Limits are per work unit** (all continuations of the unit); successful resets = continuations with a confirmed new session; failed creations counted separately. No-progress compares ProgressKey (completed, remaining, blocking, next_action; summary excluded) with the previous continuation of the unit; growth of `remaining` is recorded as scope_expanded only.
8. **Snapshots:** both paths create a new continuation context snapshot (same unit/phase/runtime state, ORIGINAL role snapshot of the source snapshot, state files at current versions); same-session path in the SAME_SESSION step, fresh path in RESTORING_CONTEXT. Phase 14 does not transition the Phase 6 runtime state; it binds runtime_state_id (must match at dispatch) and records the version.
9. **Prompt:** CONTINUE_WORK_UNIT template through the normal composer/cache path; the continuation component gains authoritative-instruction / prior-worker-report delimiters (canonical hashes of continuation prompts change once). Same-session continuations are cached only when CacheSameSessionContinuation is on (existing Phase 10 default off).
10. **Consumption:** consumer "continuation"; INCOMPLETE results of initial dispatches and every result of a continuation dispatch are consumed by Phase 14. COMPLETE / BLOCKED / FAILED of a non-continuation dispatch are not consumed (later workflow phases own them).
11. **Execution:** continuations advance automatically for RUNNING projects through a managed Runner (bounded pool, lease-claimed, one transition per step, drained on shutdown) — the codebase has no prior scheduler; Phase 16 may replace the trigger. Events reuse worker_events (00088 types).
12. **Derived files:** CONTINUATION_STATE is rendered from DB on demand (API), not written as a state-file version; PROJECT_STATE / PHASE_STATE have no deriver in the codebase, so continuation position is exposed through the read model only (14.74/14.75 verified as "progress unchanged" + read-model accuracy).

## Implementation notes (2026-10-08, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- **Lifecycle edges added to Decision 2:** READY → RESET_REQUIRED (session lost at dispatch time / operator fresh override), READY → RECOVERY_REQUIRED (readiness: binding or limits), DISPATCHING → READY (admission failed, definitely not sent → retry). Everything else as planned; FAILED / CANCELLED from every nonterminal; DB guard rejects reopening a terminal continuation and any binding change.
- **Lost-session classification:** SESSION_LOST / NO_SESSION is fresh-session recovery whether not sent or answered by the gateway (it confirms nothing executed); only DELIVERY_UNKNOWN is ambiguous → RECOVERY_REQUIRED, never resent. (`RecoverySessionLostAfterSend` enum kept, unused.)
- **Limits:** CONTINUATION_LIMIT is checked when a continuation dispatch's INCOMPLETE result is consumed — the finishing continuation goes WAITING → RECOVERY_REQUIRED and no new continuation is created (T14.37.2 / 14.112); NO_PROGRESS and RESET_LIMIT are checked in DECIDE (the new continuation is recorded, then RECOVERY_REQUIRED with no external side effect); the reset limit is also checked when a dispatch-time context-limit / lost session sends a continuation back to RESET_REQUIRED. No-progress streak = consecutive materially identical reports (first = 1; the third identical report stops at max 3). Successful resets are counted from history (CREATING_SESSION → RESTORING_CONTEXT), so a continuation that resets twice counts twice; failed creations are `session_create_failures`.
- **Telemetry:** ReadingForDispatch uses only the source dispatch's Phase 12 observations (no session fallback); if the source session is no longer the active one, the reading is treated as unknown.
- **Snapshots:** `ExecutionSnapshotService.CreateContinuationSnapshot` copies the source snapshot's role snapshot + state key / machine and re-pins state files at current versions; a runtime / phase / unit mismatch → RECOVERY_REQUIRED (BINDING_CHANGED).
- **Dispatch attempt numbering:** a continuation dispatch uses attempt = continuation attempt + 1, probing the next number on a Phase 12 idempotency conflict (an identical captured status can legitimately hit the same cached worker prompt).
- **Hooks:** `ResultService.WithOutcomeObserver` (every parse outcome; nil result when rejected), `WorkerService.WithFailureObserver` (FAILED / CANCELLED / UNKNOWN persisted), `WorkerService.ConfirmSession` (reconcile only, never creates / sends). The continuation row is inserted before its consumption row (FK), both in one transaction.
- **Runner:** `ContinuationService.Start(poll, workers)` — config `continuation.disabled / poll_interval / workers` (env OVERSEER_CONTINUATION_DISABLED / OVERSEER_CONTINUATION_POLL_INTERVAL); `Run` claims the lease, ≤ 32 transitions per claim, releases; `ProcessProject` is the synchronous explicit trigger; `Shutdown` drains before `WorkerService.Close`. Integration rigs disable the background runner (deterministic) except TestP14129 (chain through the real runner). Test seam: `SetStepHook`.
- **Pause / cancel:** pause writes nothing — every step exits before its side effect when the project is not RUNNING; responses arriving while paused are parsed and consumed (next continuation stays PENDING). Cancel: lifecycle hook cancels all nonterminal continuations (Step also cancels if it sees CANCELLED); an in-flight result is still consumed, never continued.
- **Overrides:** same_session only before the decision (PENDING / DECIDING; unsafe → 409); fresh from SAME_SESSION / READY moves to RESET_REQUIRED immediately (the new session is only created when the runner executes); otherwise recorded and applied at READY.
- **Prompt:** delimiters `<authoritative-continuation-instruction>` / `<prior-worker-report>`; delimiter-like worker text escaped as `&lt;…`; next action labelled "worker-reported, advisory". Existing Phase 8 phrases kept, so no Phase 8-10 expectation changed.
- **Precompute (14.34):** no code change — CONTINUE candidates are never FULLY_KNOWN and no precomputed template / prompt carries a prior status (verified by TestP1434).
- **Read model / API:** GET current-continuation returns `{continuation, view}` (404 `no_active_continuation`); history returns transitions + the unit's attempts + the continuation's dispatches; GET .../continuations/{id}/state renders CONTINUATION_STATE.
- **Open flag:** project_settings.auto_continue (Phase 3) is not consulted by the runner; Phase 15/16 should decide whether it gates automatic continuation.
- Test support: hermestest `FailNewSessions(n)`; P7-05 down-migration list drops `worker_dispatches.continuation_id`, consumptions, transitions, continuations first.
