Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain; internal/execution is a pure domain package (no database / web / gateway / os/exec imports); git access only through `execution.RepositoryInspector` (internal/repoinspect).
- Bootstrap is deterministic: no LLM output decides readiness, routing or success (compression is the only model call and only in initial-prompt preparation).
- Readiness is non-mutating and returns every detectable failure together; failed readiness writes nothing except an execution_events row with a null execution.
- Every stage advance is a versioned CAS + stage-history row in one transaction; events are appended in that transaction and logged after commit; a CAS conflict emits nothing.
- Project stays READY until Activate; Activate aligns project + execution in one transaction.
- Hard boundary: no worker_sessions / worker_dispatches / continuations rows, no work_units / phases / execution_plans / wbs_versions / phase document writes; tests assert row equality before/after (new writes: execution tables, runtime_state + history, state-file versions, materialized files, context snapshot, canonical / compressed / worker prompts, cache entries, precompute jobs).
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00089 project_execution_settings; 00090 executions; 00091 execution_configuration_snapshots; 00092 execution_bootstrap_transitions; 00093 runtime / history execution binding; 00094 execution_repository_baselines; 00095 execution_bootstrap_checkpoints; 00096 execution_events. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration (88 → current max); extend the P7-05 down-migration drop list if new tables reference dropped tables.
- Tests never contact a real Hermes Gateway or LLM; zero-worker assertions use the hermestest fake's operation log; git tests use temp repositories only.

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Package layout:** domain in the existing internal/execution package; git inspection in new infra package internal/repoinspect (read-only git CLI, GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 so `status` never refreshes the index). Bootstrap services live in internal/service and take no WorkerService (only read-only provider / settings lookups), so a worker call is impossible by construction.
2. **Execution identity:** Version starts at 1, Generation 1, current_session_id NULL; numbers come from projects.execution_seq and are never reused. The recovery marker is the status (RECOVERY_REQUIRED), not an extra column. Phase 14 continuation counters are read from continuation rows (zero at bootstrap); no new counter columns.
3. **Execution status edges:** INITIALIZING → READY / FAILED / CANCELLED; READY → RUNNING / FAILED / CANCELLED; RUNNING → PAUSED / RECOVERY_REQUIRED / COMPLETED / FAILED / CANCELLED; PAUSED → RUNNING / FAILED / CANCELLED; RECOVERY_REQUIRED → RUNNING / FAILED / CANCELLED; terminal: COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED. Phase 15 only uses INITIALIZING → READY → RUNNING and INITIALIZING → FAILED. One active execution per project: partial unique index + project advisory lock.
4. **Routing:** the current phase is the lowest-order non-DONE phase. Plan state: no active plan → MISSING; plan WBS version ≠ current → STALE; GENERATING / FAILED → INVALID (routes to planning); otherwise CURRENT. Next unit: the lowest-sequence in-progress unit (EXECUTING / INCOMPLETE / VALIDATING), else the first dependency-ready PENDING / READY unit (`ListEligible`); non-DONE units with none executable → WBS_INVALID. At each control state the resolver tries outcome tokens in order: (a) PLAN_REQUIRED (missing / stale / invalid) or PLAN_READY (current plan with a next unit) or WORK_COMPLETE (current plan with all units DONE), (b) READY when a next unit exists, (c) PROCEED. The first token with an enabled outgoing transition wins; none → STATE_MACHINE_INVALID. The default template gains the three PROJECT_INIT edges (P15-21). A custom machine without them follows PROCEED.
5. **Required templates:** every reachable state with a template key needs an enabled project-visible template with a current version. The default machine's keys without a template (PHASE_PLAN, RESPONSE_EVALUATE, CONTINUE_DECISION, MORE_WORK, MORE_PHASES) get global state-reference seeds (P15-22), following the existing PLAN_VALIDATE / PHASE_VALIDATE precedent.
6. **Roles:** reachable non-control states with role assignments need at least one enabled assigned role that passes CheckRoleReadiness. States without assignments use the Phase 7 fallback (smallest-key enabled role). The captured roles are the snapshots of every role assigned to a reachable state, plus the fallback role.
7. **Runtime:** runtime_state stays one row per project and gains execution_id. Bind rules: a row for this execution is reused; an unbound row (Phase 6 prepare) is adopted and reset; a row bound to a terminal execution is rebound and reset; history is preserved. Bootstrap applies the control path, so the runtime ends at the first actionable state with its phase / unit. History rows carry execution_id, starting with a BOOTSTRAP entry (NULL → initial state).
8. **Consistency (no SERIALIZABLE):** readiness + plan + snapshot are built from reads, then the create transaction (project advisory lock) re-reads SourceVersions. A mismatch writes nothing and reruns readiness (max 3 attempts, then CONCURRENT_BOOTSTRAP). After the snapshot is committed, context derivation uses captured versions where the API allows (template version, machine binding). FINALIZING cross-checks the context snapshot's role config hash, the template version, the WBS version and the machine binding against the configuration snapshot. A mismatch fails the execution as CONFIGURATION, and Bootstrap reruns once from readiness (new execution number).
9. **Failure policy:** CONFIGURATION codes (PROJECT_NOT_READY, CONFIG_INVALID, WORKDIR_INVALID, REPOSITORY_INVALID, NO_PHASE, WBS_INVALID, STATE_MACHINE_INVALID, ROLE_INVALID, STATE_FILE_INVALID, PROMPT_INVALID) → FAILED (terminal) once an execution exists. RECOVERABLE (MATERIALIZATION_FAILED, COMPRESSION_FAILED, CONCURRENT_BOOTSTRAP) and INFRASTRUCTURE (PERSISTENCE_FAILED) keep the execution INITIALIZING with stage FAILED + failed_stage, and a retry resumes it (same id). The project stays READY in every failure case.
10. **Snapshot boundary:** the configuration snapshot is committed in the same transaction as the execution row (after readiness, before runtime initialization). From then on, project edits never affect the execution.
11. **Bootstrap entry semantics:** POST /bootstrap = Bootstrap → VerifyBootstrapCheckpoint → Activate.
    - Project RUNNING with a RUNNING active execution → 200 with the same execution (idempotent).
    - READY project + READY execution → activate.
    - INITIALIZING held by another live claim → 409 CONCURRENT_BOOTSTRAP with its id.
    - INITIALIZING with stage FAILED or an expired claim → resume.
    - Readiness failure → 422 report. Other project statuses → 409 PROJECT_NOT_READY. All phases DONE → 409 NO_PHASE with completion_eligible = true, nothing written.
12. **Legacy POST /start is unchanged** (Phase 3 lifecycle-only READY → RUNNING without an execution); dozens of Phase 3–14 tests depend on it. **Open decision:** route /start through bootstrap, or retire it (Phase 16 / 17).
13. **State files:** the bootstrap creates or versions PROJECT_STATE and TASK_ARCHIVE (project), PHASE_STATE and TEST_STATE (current phase), and ACTIVE_TASK only with a resolved unit. ARCHITECTURE is never created. A new version is written only when the content hash differs. TEST_STATE starts as "No deterministic validation recorded", because no persisted test records exist before Phase 16. Materialization is opt-in (materialize_state_files, default false) into <canonical CWD>/.overseer/state.
14. **Execution settings** (00089): require_git_repository false, dirty_policy WARN, materialize_state_files false, max_bootstrap_control_transitions 100, bootstrap lease 60 s. The allowed-roots policy is the configured worker provider's `allowed_roots` (server policy; empty = unrestricted).
15. **Secrets:** the worker snapshot stores the provider name / kind, endpoint with userinfo stripped, timeouts, max response bytes, capabilities, worker settings and the auth secret's env-var NAME only. The compression snapshot stores the provider, model, settings and config hash (api_key_env name only). Database credentials are never captured.
16. **Initial prompt:** composed through PromptCacheService.PrepareForContext with the template version captured in the snapshot. The precompute kickoff is PrecomputeService.OnTrigger(PROJECT_START) after the initial prompt; it is not awaited and does not affect bootstrap.
17. **Events:** a new execution_events table (not worker_events). A readiness failure is EXECUTION_BOOTSTRAP_FAILED with a null execution. Metrics are derived from events, so they count committed outcomes exactly once.
18. **Startup recovery:** interrupted (non-FAILED, claim-expired) INITIALIZING executions resume automatically, and a READY execution of a READY project is activated. FAILED-stage executions wait for an explicit retry (POST /bootstrap).
19. **Checkpoint verification** relies on immutable artifacts (configuration snapshot, bootstrap history rows, state-file versions, context snapshot, baselines, worker prompt), so it stays valid after Phase 16 moves the runtime.
20. **Not in Phase 15:** mirroring project pause / resume / cancel onto the active execution, and consulting project_settings.auto_continue. Both are flagged for Phase 16. Phase 16 must also compose later prompts with the execution snapshot's captured template / role versions.

## Implementation notes (2026-10-08, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- **Readiness vs Phase 6 validation:** the seeded default workflow has two dead-end states (COMPLETE, INCOMPLETE) that are unreachable from PROJECT_INIT, which Phase 6 validation flags. Validation issues confined to states unreachable from the initial state are reported as readiness **warnings**. Issues on reachable states remain blocking STATE_MACHINE_INVALID errors.
- **Response protocol pin (added readiness check):** the Phase 8 composer requires every prompted state to pin a response protocol (`state_protocol_references`). Readiness therefore reports `PROMPT_INVALID` (category response_protocol) for every reachable state with a template key and no pinned protocol. The default workflow carries no pins, so projects must configure them (the p15 rig pins the Phase 13 IMPLEMENTATION protocol on every prompted state).
- **Role readiness** uses `RoleService.ValidateRole`, which requires configured allowed states. An enabled assigned role failing validation is `ROLE_INVALID` ("X role is invalid: …"); a reachable state whose assigned roles are all disabled or missing is `ROLE_INVALID` ("STATE requires role X, which is disabled").
- **Stage/status history:** one table (`execution_bootstrap_transitions`, with a `status` column) records both stage advances and status changes, so READY and RUNNING are separate audited rows (15.83). `execution_events` gained `seq BIGSERIAL` for a deterministic event order.
- **Concurrency:** requests are serialized four ways.
  - An in-process per-project mutex covers requests within one instance (they share the claim owner).
  - The DB claim (owner + lease from the execution settings) covers multiple instances.
  - A project advisory lock plus the active-execution check run inside the create transaction.
  - The partial unique index is the final backstop.
  
  A CAS conflict during a stage means another instance advanced the execution. It returns `CONCURRENT_BOOTSTRAP` and records nothing.
- **Source re-check:** inside the create transaction (after the advisory lock), every source is re-gathered and the SourceVersions are diffed. A mismatch writes nothing and reruns readiness. Test seams: `SetCreateHook` (between readiness and commit) and `SetStageHook` (before each stage).
- **Context before prompt:** the initial context snapshot id is recorded (`SetArtifacts`) before prompt preparation, so a compression-failure retry reuses the same snapshot (no recomposition of context). The Phase 10 negative cache (default 30 s) can delay an immediate retry; the integration test sets the project's negative window to 0.
- **Checkpoint:** the domain checkpoint gained `BootstrapHistoryCount` and `ContextSnapshotHash`. Its state-file refs are the version ids of the derived files plus ARCHITECTURE when present. They are captured at finalization and verified by version id (immutable rows), so verification stays valid after later edits.
- **Drift handling (Decision 8)** is implemented as specified. Cross-checks cover the context role config hash vs the snapshot, the machine binding, the current WBS version, and the active plan id. On drift, execution #N fails as `CONFIG_INVALID` and one automatic rerun creates #N+1 (tests: WBS v4→v5 and a PLANNER prompt change mid-bootstrap).
- **Materialization:** Phase 7 `Materialize` only writes PROJECT-scoped files (PROJECT_STATE, TASK_ARCHIVE, ARCHITECTURE) under `<CWD>/.overseer/state`.
- **Records:** `RuntimeStateRecord` / `TransitionHistoryRecord` gained `ExecutionID`. History readers COALESCE the bootstrap entry's NULL `from_state_id` to `uuid.Nil`. Runtime `state_machine_id` is the machine family id (as in Phase 6 `Initialize`).
- **API:** malformed path ids return 404 (existing `pathUUIDs` convention). The 400 case is invalid paging / settings. POST /bootstrap returns 201 (bootstrapped) or 200 (already running / activated / resumed). Readiness failure → 422 with `readiness.report` + `readiness.view`. PROJECT_NOT_READY / NO_PHASE / CONCURRENT_BOOTSTRAP → 409.
- **Config:** `bootstrap.recovery_disabled` (env `OVERSEER_BOOTSTRAP_RECOVERY_DISABLED`); integration rigs disable startup recovery except the restart tests.
- **PROJECT_STATE** is rendered at file initialization (execution status INITIALIZING). Derived files are not re-rendered on activation; Phase 16 owns later updates.
- **Speculative isolation (15.125)** is tested with a blocking precompute hook (bootstrap completes while the job is blocked: not awaited) and with precompute disabled (trigger refused, bootstrap succeeds). A failing candidate cannot be forced through a public seam without panicking outside the job's recover.
- **Live check** covered the REST surface on a fresh database (schema 96, settings, readiness / preview, 409 / 422 / 404 / 405, events, metrics, graceful shutdown). The successful bootstrap path (git repository, seeded workflow) is covered by the integration suite.

## Open decisions (from PLAN.md)
- Decision 12: legacy POST /start still moves READY → RUNNING without an execution — route it through bootstrap or retire it.
- Decision 20: project pause / resume / cancel are not mirrored onto the active execution; project_settings.auto_continue is not consulted; Phase 16 must compose later prompts with the execution snapshot's captured template / role versions.
- New: the default workflow carries no state → response-protocol pins, and projects must pin one per prompted state before bootstrap readiness passes. Seed default pins, or keep this as explicit project configuration?

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain. internal/execution, internal/wbs, internal/hardening are pure domain packages (no database / web / gateway / os/exec / go/build tooling). Process execution only in internal/valrun, git writes only in internal/gitcheckpoint, Go source analysis only in internal/goanalysis, source rewriting only in internal/gorefactor.
- Workers report, the engine decides: no worker output marks a unit / phase / project DONE, selects a strategy, or replaces deterministic validation. Worker claims (tests, acceptance) are never validation inputs.
- Every step: lease epoch check → read → intent row (committed) → external action → result row → transition (runtime + execution CAS + event in one tx). A CAS conflict writes nothing. No external action without a committed intent.
- No blind resend: a dispatch whose delivery is unknown goes to RECOVERY_REQUIRED.
- Engine uses only WorkerService / ResultService / ContinuationService (no gateway, no Hermes types).
- Hardening never edits excluded source, never uses regex rewriting, and keeps a REQUIRED baseline checkpoint.
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00097 project_engine_settings; 00098 executions engine columns; 00099 execution_steps; 00100 execution_events exec_seq; 00101 work-unit / plan / plan_candidates; 00102 phase DONE trigger; 00103 validation / fix / approvals; 00104 repository_checkpoints; 00105 hardening tables; 00106 execution_completions. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration (96 → current max); extend the P7-05 down-migration drop list.
- Tests never contact a real Hermes Gateway or LLM; git / Go-module tests use temp repositories only; validation commands in integration are deterministic.

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Package layout:** engine domain extends internal/execution; plan candidates extend internal/wbs; new domain package internal/hardening; new infra packages internal/valrun, internal/gitcheckpoint, internal/goanalysis, internal/gorefactor (stdlib go/parser, go/ast, go/types, go/format + `go list`; no new module dependency). Engine services live in internal/service.
2. **Statuses:** WBS PENDING = Phase 15 INITIALIZING / READY. WAITING is added (worker or continuation in flight). Schedulable: RUNNING, and WAITING whose condition has resolved.
3. **Driver lease:** separate from the bootstrap claim: driver_owner / driver_lease_until / driver_epoch (fencing token incremented on takeover; every side effect checks it).
4. **Action resolution:** built-in bindings for the default-workflow keys (PHASE_PLAN → PLAN_DISPATCH, PLAN_VALIDATE → PLAN_VALIDATE, WORK_UNIT_PREPARE → SELECT_UNIT, IMPLEMENT / FIX / CONTINUE → DISPATCH, RESPONSE_EVALUATE → EVALUATE_RESULT, CONTINUE_DECISION / SAME_SESSION / RESET_SESSION → CONTINUATION, VALIDATE → RUN_UNIT_VALIDATION, UNIT_DONE → UNIT_GATES, MORE_WORK → MORE_WORK, PHASE_VALIDATE → PHASE_VALIDATION, MORE_PHASES → MORE_PHASES, FINAL_HARDENING, HARDENING_FIX, FINAL_VALIDATE, FINAL_CHECKPOINT, PROJECT_DONE → COMPLETE), and other states fall back by kind. The unit checkpoint is part of UNIT_GATES (no new CHECKPOINT state).
5. **Default workflow changes (P16-78):** BLOCKED has no default edge, so it goes to RECOVERY_REQUIRED. Worker FAILED → FIX (bounded by the fix limit). Phase validation FAIL → PHASE_PLAN (amendment). The last phase goes through FINAL_HARDENING → FINAL_VALIDATE → FINAL_CHECKPOINT → PROJECT_DONE. Seeded machines in existing databases are not migrated; new projects copy the new template.
6. **Atomic policy specifics:** "separate composition / integration" = COMPOSITION / INTEGRATION units must declare dependencies and must not also be the sole unit of the plan. "One worker invocation" = an explicit `single_invocation: true` declaration. "Independently testable" = at least one validation command or required test.
7. **Planner output:** a new optional `plan` object in the Phase 13 v1 result (raw JSON kept on WorkerResult and covered by the normalized hash). A missing or malformed plan on a planning dispatch is a rejected candidate, never a plan.
8. **Validation sources:** unit commands = unit spec `validation` ∪ engine settings UnitValidation; phase = PhaseValidation; final = FinalValidation. An empty resolved set is NOT_CONFIGURED, which goes to recovery (not PASS). A timeout is FAIL with timed_out = true. An exec / CWD / start error is INFRA_ERROR. FIX evidence reaches the worker through the TEST_STATE derived file.
9. **Continuations:** Phase 14 keeps ownership of continuation decisions and continuation dispatches. The engine waits on them (WAITING) and maps the strategy onto the SAME / RESET edges. The next IMPLEMENT step adopts the continuation's dispatch instead of dispatching again.
10. **Plan amendment:** a new plan version (kind AMENDMENT) carries the DONE units as copies (carried_from_unit_id, status DONE) plus the repair units. The prior plan becomes SUPERSEDED and stays unchanged. Progress = DONE / total over the current plan, computed, never stored.
11. **Hardening work representation:** hardening operations are persisted in hardening_operations (not work_units), so WBS progress is untouched (16.145). They run through the same step engine, ValidationService, fix attempts, CheckpointService and recovery. Mechanical edits (copy, test generation, reference migration, removal) are done by internal/gorefactor (language-aware, deterministic). The worker is used only for HARDENING_FIX.
12. **Checkpoints:** commits carry `Overseer-Execution` / `Overseer-Checkpoint: <key>` trailers. Reconciliation finds them by key, so a crash after commit never duplicates. The HARDENING_BASELINE checkpoint is always REQUIRED. The other checkpoints follow CheckpointPolicy (default REQUIRED). DISABLED never touches git.
13. **Manual acceptance criteria:** REVIEW_REQUIRED → RECOVERY_REQUIRED. The operator approves through POST /recovery/resolve (APPROVE_CRITERIA), which records acceptance_approvals.
14. **Lifecycle mirroring (resolves Phase 15 Decision 20):** project Pause / Resume / Cancel hooks mirror onto the active execution. project_settings.auto_continue = false → the scheduler skips the project (manual POST /step still works). Prompts use the snapshot's captured role / template versions.
15. **Legacy POST /start (Phase 15 Decision 12):** unchanged in Phase 16; an execution-less RUNNING project is never scheduled. Retire it in Phase 17 / 18.
16. **Completion:** one transaction sets execution COMPLETED, project COMPLETED (TransitionTx), the execution_completions row and the PROJECT_COMPLETED event. COMPLETED executions are immutable (DB trigger). Reopening = a new execution generation.

## Implementation notes (2026-10-08, recorded deviations / task-level choices)
- **Statuses / active set:** WAITING is active and schedulable. The dbrepo active-execution SQL and the partial unique index include it.
- **Engine settings (P16-02):** the planned `ManualCriteria` setting was dropped. An unapproved MANUAL criterion always means REVIEW_REQUIRED → RECOVERY_REQUIRED, and an operator approves through `POST /recovery/resolve` (APPROVE_CRITERIA).
- **Atomic policy specifics (Decision 6 revised):**
  - "Explicit dependencies": COMPOSITION / INTEGRATION / VALIDATION units declare at least one dependency.
  - "Separate composition": no ATOMIC unit depends on a COMPOSITION unit.
  - "Separate integration": no ATOMIC / COMPOSITION unit depends on an INTEGRATION unit.
  - Amendment candidates may depend on carried unit keys (`ValidateAmendmentStructure`).
- **Validation outcomes:**
  - A timeout is FAIL (`timed_out`); an exec / CWD / start error is INFRA_ERROR.
  - An empty resolved command set (NOT_CONFIGURED) is a recovery condition for unit and phase scope.
  - Commands run in their own process group (a timeout kills children) with a filtered environment (no secrets).
- **FIX evidence (16.49):** the composer renders only the state files of required types. Rather than making TEST_STATE a machine-wide requirement (which would break Phase 7–14 snapshots), the engine pins TEST_STATE as an optional extra file through `ExecutionSnapshotService.CreateExecutionSnapshotIncluding`, for FIX / HARDENING_FIX dispatches only. ACTIVE_TASK (the task definition) is untouched.
- **BLOCKED:** the unit keeps its in-progress status (not BLOCKED), so a resolved recovery can resume it. The evidence is recorded on the execution.
- **Dispatch adoption (Decision 9):** a terminal, not-yet-evaluated dispatch from a continuation, or made in another action state (FIX → IMPLEMENT), is adopted by the next action state instead of being re-dispatched. Crash reconciliation finds a step's dispatch by (prompt, runtime, created after the intent). A dispatch whose delivery is unknown goes to recovery. A DEFINITELY_NOT_SENT one is retried with attempt + 1.
- **Continuations:** the engine drives the Phase 14 continuation of the evaluated dispatch synchronously (`ContinuationService.Run`) and maps its strategy onto SAME / RESET. Phase 14 keeps the decision, the reset, the CWD restore and the continuation dispatch.
- **Plan reuse (16.11):** PHASE_PLAN / PLAN_VALIDATE pass through (SUBMIT / ACCEPTED, `reuse_plan`) when the working phase has a current plan with remaining work.
- **Final-validation repair:** a failed final validation routes FINAL_HARDENING → HARDENING_FIX (bounded by MaxFixAttempts) whether or not hardening is enabled. Without this, a disabled-hardening project would loop between the two states.
- **Hardening specifics:**
  - The operation kinds include CHECKPOINT (the per-group checkpoint, 16.133).
  - Methods are never auto-consolidated: a used receiver → UNSAFE_TYPE_DEPENDENCY; an unused receiver → REQUIRES_REVIEW (no shared abstraction).
  - Package-local dependencies → UNSAFE_TYPE_DEPENDENCY.
  - Settings gained `domain_packages` / `infra_packages` (layers for 16.102 / 16.106).
  - Round control: the analysis of round max + 1 only reports. A SAFE group found there is REMAINING and the termination is MAX_ROUNDS with a warning.
  - The report counts distinct groups (latest round per key); every round's records are kept.
  - A failed operation is retried only after a HARDENING_FIX dispatched since the failure.
  - An operation left RUNNING by a crash is reconciled from the source, and the repository fingerprint is re-baselined.
  - The external-modification guard runs before every other operation.
  - Copying into a package the body already uses drops the self-qualifier and the self-import.
- **Planner output (Decision 7):** `worker_results.plan` is TEXT (added in 00101), so the bytes covered by the normalized hash round-trip exactly. Results without a plan keep their Phase 13 hash.
- **Events (16.82):** `AppendEvent` assigns `exec_seq` under an execution-scoped advisory lock (inside the caller's transaction, or a short own transaction). Phase 15 bootstrap events get sequences too.
- **Phase DONE guard (00102):** enforced for projects that have an execution. Phases 2–7 data-level tests mark phases DONE directly without executions, and remain valid bookkeeping.
- **P7-15 test fix (gate):** `TestP715ConcurrentMaterializationNeverPartial` registered each new complete version only after writing it, so a racer could materialize it first. This flaked deterministically under `-race`. It now registers the content before the write (test-only change).
- **Gate timeout:** the `-race` suite now exceeds Go's 10-minute default (integration ≈ 507 s), so gates run `go test -race -timeout 40m ./...`.
- **COMPLETED immutability (00106):** the guard rejects every column change on a COMPLETED execution except a reference cleared to NULL by an `ON DELETE SET NULL` cascade (project / phase deletion still works).
- **Concurrency:** a CAS conflict caused by a concurrent pause / cancel / recovery ends the step quietly with the new status (the step stays INTENT and resumes). Two engines on one execution: one drives, the other gets ErrExecutionBusy.
- **Config:**
  - `engine.scheduler_disabled` / `recovery_disabled` / `interval` / `workers` (env `OVERSEER_ENGINE_*`).
  - The integration harness disables the scheduler by default; tests opt in.
  - The scheduler drives only projects with `auto_continue = true` (DB default false).
- **API:** pause / resume / cancel are project lifecycle operations (idempotent pause). Execution DTOs gained an `engine` object (position, wait / recovery reason and evidence, driver, generation).
- **Template / seed pins:** the default workflow is now 28 states / 38 transitions (P6-11 pins); global templates 18 (P8-12 pins). P6-39 walks the new finalization path. The schema pin is 106.
- **Test seams:** `Engine.SetStepHook` (stages `intent`, `dispatch`, `dispatched`, `validation`, `checkpoint`, `checkpoint-committed`, `hardening:<op>`, `hardening-done:<op>`), `NewEngineForTest` (second instance), `BootstrapService.AppendEngineEventForTest`.

# PHASE 16 COMPLETE

Phase 16 (Autonomous Project Execution Engine & Final Hardening) is implemented — P16-01..P16-162 DONE in PLAN.md; deviations recorded under "Implementation notes".

## PHASE 17 — Web Interface, Operations Console & Human Control Plane (PLAN + completion record, archived 2026-10-09)

# PLAN — Phase 17: Web Interface, Operations Console & Human Control Plane

Authority: /go/app/src/Phase 17 Web Interface.md (WBS §17.1–17.200 + DoD L3802-3909). Project root: /go/app/src (Go module `overseer`).
Decomposition: bottom-up (primitive → composition → integration → validation), one behavior per task, WBS TDD tests exist before implementation.
Inherited (Phase 16 COMPLETE 2026-10-08; record in TASK_ARCHIVE.md): JSON API in internal/web (Router.RegisterAPI, `{"error":{code,message,fields},"request_id"}` model, RequestID / Recovery / RequestLog middleware, Renderer over web/templates base / home / error), ~150 /api/v1 routes (projects incl. ?status / limit / offset, settings with project-version CAS, phases (append-only POST), WBS (PUT expected_version, versions, diff), current-wbs, summary, readiness, roles / instructions / preview / system-prompt versions, state machine (+ validation, versions, states, transitions), state files (+ versions, drift, materialize), prompts (preview, provenance, history, compression attempts / diff, worker-prompt lineage), prompt cache, precompute, worker sessions / dispatches / results / parse attempts, continuations, executions (bootstrap, bootstrap-preview, execution-readiness, configuration snapshot, metrics), engine (step, pause / resume / cancel, recovery/resolve, timeline, steps, validations, checkpoints, summary, hardening, settings/engine)), `BootstrapService`, `Engine` + scheduler, `LifecycleService`, `StateFileMaterializeService.Import` / DetectDrift, `PromptTemplateService` (global seeds, CurrentVersion), `prompt.ErrUnknownVariable`, execution_events (global `seq`, per-execution `exec_seq` under advisory lock), repoinspect (read-only git), p16 integration rig + hermestest fake.
Phase 17 adds the server-rendered operations console, the read models it needs, live updates (SSE), and the web security / audit boundary. The browser displays, configures and requests actions; Go services decide.
Test PG: postgres://pguser:***@172.17.0.1:5432/devpipe?sslmode=disable (override OVERSEER_TEST_PG_DSN).

Rules:
- Execution order follows the dependency chain. internal/access, internal/audit, internal/readmodel are pure domain packages (no database / net/http / gateway / os/exec).
- The browser never decides: allowed actions, readiness, staleness, progress, unit stage and recovery actions are computed in Go and rendered; JS only refetches server-rendered fragments and manages the connection indicator.
- Every mutation passes Session → Authenticate → CSRF → Authorize → service → Audit. GET / HEAD never mutate. No endpoint sets workflow / unit / phase / project state generically.
- Untrusted content (WBS, prompts, worker output, validation output, filenames, paths, commands) is rendered only through html/template contexts; no `template.HTML` on such data.
- Secrets: never stored by Phase 17, never returned; Redactor applies to API metadata, audit metadata and SSE payloads.
- Read models use bounded query counts; list endpoints paginate server-side; large text is range-loaded.
- Migration numbering (verify max+1 at execution): 00107 audit_events; 00108 execution_events.project_seq; 00109 state_file_drift_acknowledgements; 00110 read-model indexes. Bump `version != NN` pins in internal/integration (106 → current max); extend the P7-05 down-migration drop list.
- Tests never contact a real Hermes Gateway or LLM; no browser / JS runtime exists on the box — "browser" tests use the p17 Go client (cookie jar, CSRF, forms, SSE).

## Decisions taken in this plan (flag to ARCHITECT/user if any should change)
1. **Package layout:** the existing JSON API stays in internal/web (package `web`). New server-rendered pages live in internal/web/handlers with consumer-side interfaces; middleware in internal/web/middleware; template view models in internal/web/viewmodels; templates and static assets are embedded from internal/web/templates and internal/web/static (the repo-root web/ directory is retired). Read-model DTOs live in the new pure package internal/readmodel, so handlers never import internal/service. Existing API handlers keep their concrete-service registration (T17.1.2 is enforced for the new handlers package).
2. **UI technology:** server-rendered html/template + two small vanilla JS files (forms.js, live.js), no framework, no new module dependency. Every page is complete without JS; live updates refetch server-rendered fragments, so the browser holds no authoritative or arithmetic state (17.77 / 17.78 hold by construction).
3. **Authentication v1:** `LocalAuthenticator` → LOCAL_ADMIN principal; the Authenticator / Authorizer interfaces (internal/access + middleware) are wired on every route, so later user auth swaps the implementation only.
4. **CSRF:** HMAC session-bound tokens for cookie-bearing or form requests. Cookie-less JSON API calls (programmatic clients, existing integration tests) pass only without a cross-site Origin / Sec-Fetch-Site. Session cookie is SameSite=Strict, HttpOnly, Secure (configurable for plain-http local use).
5. **Error model:** the existing envelope is kept; `details` [{field, message}] is added beside `fields`. Validation stays 400 (established convention, 17.134 allows it); codes stay lower-snake for existing errors; new codes per P17-06.
6. **SSE source:** execution_events gain a per-project, commit-ordered `project_seq` (advisory xact lock), used as the SSE `id` / Last-Event-ID, so gaps are detectable. A single in-process poller per subscribed project feeds bounded subscriber buffers; slow subscribers are disconnected and resume via Last-Event-ID. No LISTEN / NOTIFY.
7. **Audit scope:** audit_events records human / API-originated mutations (actor, action, resource, result, reason, request id; redacted metadata), written by the audit middleware after the service call (best-effort with ERROR log on write failure, never blocking the action). Autonomous engine history stays in the execution timeline (execution_events); the audit page links to it.
8. **Secrets (17.59 / 17.122 / 17.123):** provider credentials remain environment references in server config (WBS 9.41 / 12.94). The UI shows "configured / not configured" and offers no secret value field, so a blank field can never erase a secret. Replacing a secret is an operator action on the environment. **Confirm with the user** if an in-app secret store is wanted (would need encryption-key management; Phase 18 candidate).
9. **Recovery actions:** typed set RECONCILE_DISPATCH, RUN_VALIDATION, RETRY_CHECKPOINT, APPROVE_CRITERIA, MANUAL_RESUME mapped onto existing Phase 12 / 16 operations. REPLAN is not offered (no backend operation exists; Phase 16 replans automatically on stale / failed plans). DELIVERY_UNKNOWN never maps to an action that dispatches.
10. **Drift "Ignore":** acknowledgement per (state file, filesystem hash), new table 00109; a later filesystem change re-raises drift.
11. **Start control:** the UI uses Phase 15 bootstrap / activation only. Legacy POST /start stays as-is (Phase 15 Decision 12 / Phase 16 Decision 15); retire in Phase 18.
12. **Project creation:** ProjectSetupService validates every field first and returns all errors; it creates the project and applies settings sequentially with compensation (delete) on later failure.
13. **Config concurrency:** every settings PUT requires the echoed project `version` (P17-46); endpoints lacking it change contract (recorded per endpoint).

## Implementation notes
- **Migrations:** 00107 audit_events, 00108 execution_events.project_seq, 00109 state_file_drift_acknowledgements, 00110 read-model indexes, plus **00111 projects.config_version** (not in the plan: the per-project configuration version behind the settings guard, P17-46). Integration pins 106 → 111; the P7-05 down list also drops audit_events and state_file_drift_acknowledgements.
- **00108 backfill** orders by (created_at, exec_seq NULLS FIRST, id), not by the global `seq`: some existing databases (devpipe) lack `seq` on execution_events. The trigger is disabled during the backfill; the column then becomes NOT NULL + UNIQUE (project_id, project_seq).
- **Decision 13 softened:** a settings PUT *may* echo `version` (body member) / X-Config-Version. When present it is compare-and-set (409 when stale); when absent the write is unconditional, so existing API clients and tests keep working. Every console form always sends it. Each successful write bumps the version, and a failed write reverts the bump.
- **JSON decoders** now require a JSON object; `null` / `[]` bodies return 400 (found by the P17-129 fuzz). PREVIEW routes are excluded from the "no DB change" fuzz because they are read-only by contract.
- **Prompt preview** at a position that cannot be composed (e.g. PHASE_PLAN without captured WBS inputs) returns 409 `prompt_not_composable` instead of 500.
- **Overview:** CurrentTask is nil when the current unit is DONE (between units / finished). Context usage is computed against the execution snapshot threshold.
- **Executing-phase WBS stays protected** (Phase 3 P3-17; pinned by T3.18.7 / T4.1.6 / T4.5.5): an edit of an EXECUTING / VALIDATING / DONE phase is a 409 conflict page. P17-138 therefore covers WBS 17.190 as follows:
  - the executing phase's edit is refused;
  - a later (appended) phase's WBS is edited during execution;
  - that phase is planned from the new version (plan.source_wbs_version = 2).
  
  Stale-plan display itself is covered by the backend staleness read model (P17-50 / TestP1748) and the Phase 16 "stale WBS during planning" flow.
- **Acceptance steps 31–34 (P17-147):** no backend operation lets an execution continue past a DELIVERY_UNKNOWN dispatch. RECONCILE_DISPATCH never sends; MANUAL_RESUME re-evaluates the same dispatch and stops again (Decision 9 / 17.99). The acceptance therefore uses a resolvable stop for "approved recovery → continue": a unit with a MANUAL criterion, then APPROVE_CRITERIA with confirmation and reason through the recovery page. The DELIVERY_UNKNOWN guarantees are asserted in TestP17137: banner, evidence, only RECONCILE / MANUAL_RESUME offered, no retry, nothing resent, audited reason. See the open decision in ACTIVE_TASK.
- **Creation audit:** POST /api/v1/projects and POST /api/v1/console/projects now set `Location: /api/v1/projects/{id}`. The audit middleware takes the project id of a 201 response from that header, so PROJECT_CREATE rows appear under the project's audit filter.
- **Rate limiter defaults:** 20 req/s, burst 60 per principal for EXPENSIVE routes. The first choice (lower) throttled existing API suites (TestT4175).
- **LiveEventFeed:** bursts mark a subscriber as lagging instead of dropping it. Only a subscriber that makes no progress for StallTicks polls is disconnected (it resumes via Last-Event-ID).
- **Malformed worker output** (invalid UTF-8, oversized summaries) is a protocol violation in Phase 13. It stops in recovery and is never displayed as a result. P17-144 is therefore split:
  - TestP17141 covers hostile-but-valid content rendered as data;
  - TestP17144 covers malformed output → recovery, with pages still safe and bounded.
- **Role editor** PUTs only the changed parts (prompt / allowed states / state files), so an unchanged allowed-states list never creates a version.
- **Error mapping:** several API mappers were made consistent (not found → 404, conflict → 409). Lifecycle invalid transitions stay 409 by design.
- **Renames** to avoid collisions: execution.EventCat* (readiness already had Category*); dbrepo web* scanners; UnitDetailRow.ValidationCommands.
- The repo-root `web/` directory is retired, and the TemplatesDir / StaticDir config defaults were removed (assets are embedded). App version is 0.17.0.
- **Shutdown order (found by the live check):** App.Shutdown / Stop now stop the LiveEventFeed *before* draining the HTTP server. Previously an idle open SSE stream held the drain until the shutdown timeout (30 s live). Regression test: TestP17ShutdownClosesStreams.
- **Live install seeding:** global prompt templates and the default workflow template are still not seeded at boot (accepted Phase 6 / 8 decision: on-demand service ops). A fresh database therefore cannot take a web-created project to READY: readiness lists the missing templates. The live check seeded them with a temporary module-built helper (not committed). See the open decision.
- **Test infrastructure:** web tests run with `web.cookie_insecure=true` (plain-http test server). The p11 projects-row fingerprint helper excludes `config_version`. The p17 rig adds a browser client (cookie jar, CSRF, forms, SSE), a form scraper and a scoped crawler.


# PHASE 17 COMPLETE

Phase 17 (Web Interface) is implemented: P17-01..P17-148 are DONE in PLAN.md. Deviations are recorded under "Implementation notes".

## Evidence (2026-10-09)
- **Static checks:** gofmt -l, go vet ./... and go build ./... are all clean.
- **go test ./... -count=1:** all 37 packages ok (integration 610 s).
- **go test -race -timeout 40m ./... -count=1:** all 37 packages ok, no data races (integration 693 s).
- **archtest green:**
  - internal/access, internal/audit and internal/readmodel are domain packages;
  - internal/web/** must not import dbrepo / database / gateway / pgx;
  - handlers must not import internal/service;
  - there are no runtime-state write calls from web (T17.1.1–5).
- **Migrations 00107–00111** are applied; schema max = 111 (pins 106 → 111). The P7-05 down list drops audit_events and state_file_drift_acknowledgements.
- **Console:** about 60 server-rendered page routes plus 7 live fragments, all embedded with html/template. There are two vanilla JS files and no framework. Every GET is READ; every POST is delegated to a typed /api/v1 route, then authorized and audited.
- **Security:** headers, session, CSRF, body limits, rate limits and the redactor. P17127 (security matrix), P1772 (no generic state mutation) and P17129 (mutation fuzz: no DB change on rejection) all pass.
- **Live events:** SSE on the per-project `project_seq`, with Last-Event-ID / ?after resume and one poller per project. Slow consumers are disconnected (P17126).
- **Read models:** query counts are constant as data grows (P17130: 50 phases, 5,000 units, 100k events, 10 MB logs).
- **End to end (p17 browser client: cookie jar, CSRF, forms, SSE):**
  - P17131 creation, P17132 start / pause / resume / cancel, P17133 observed execution, P17135 context reset, P17136 pause between units, P17137 DELIVERY_UNKNOWN, P17138 WBS edit + append;
  - P17140 amendment, P17141 completion + secret scan, P17142 restart, P17144 malformed output, P17146 history;
  - TestP17147Acceptance: the 40-step 17.200 scenario through pages and APIs with the "verify throughout" assertions (268 pages crawled, 246 events, 25 audit rows).
- **Leak probe:** 0 leaked test databases. Four `*_live` / `*_probe` databases from earlier phases remain (pre-existing, untouched).
- **Live binary** (overseer_p17, DB overseer_p17_live, port 18917): 28 / 28 checks PASS.
  - Pages and DB: schema 111; 12 pages render 200.
  - Headers and session: CSP / X-Frame-Options DENY / nosniff; session cookie HttpOnly + SameSite=Strict.
  - CSRF: requests rejected without a token (cookie-bearing) and from a cross-site origin.
  - Project flow: created via the console API (201 + Location) → phase → WBS → ready → bootstrap 201.
  - SSE: stream returned 9 events; resume after Last-Event-ID 8 delivered 1 event.
  - Audit: pause / resume 200, with SUCCEEDED rows (reason recorded) for PROJECT_CREATE / PHASE_APPEND / WBS_EDIT / PROJECT_UPDATE / LIFECYCLE_START / PAUSE / RESUME.
  - Secrets: the provider secret sentinels appear in no response (about 60 KB scanned) and not in the log; the DB password is not in the log.
  - Shutdown: SIGTERM with an open stream ends with "shutdown complete" in 6 ms; the stream is closed and there are 0 ERROR lines.
  - Before the shutdown-order fix this took 30 s. The fix is regression-tested by TestP17ShutdownClosesStreams.
  - The global templates were seeded with a temporary helper (see the open decisions).

## Open decisions
- **Secrets (Decision 8):** provider secrets stay as environment references. The UI shows only configured / not configured. Is an in-app encrypted secret store wanted (Phase 18 candidate)?
- **DELIVERY_UNKNOWN has no continuation path:** RECONCILE_DISPATCH never sends; MANUAL_RESUME re-evaluates and stops again, so the only exit is cancel. Decide whether to add an operator "settle dispatch as not processed / adopt result" operation (confirmed, audited), or keep the current behaviour. The acceptance used a manual-review stop for "approved recovery → continue".
- **Executing-phase WBS protection (P3-17)** is kept. A current-phase WBS edit during execution is refused (409), so "stale plan via current-phase edit" cannot happen while the phase is EXECUTING. Relax it only if mid-phase replanning is wanted.
- **Fresh install seeding:** global prompt templates and the default workflow template are not seeded at boot, so a web-created project cannot reach READY on a new database. Decide whether to seed at startup (idempotent ops already exist) or add an admin action.
- **Settings `version` is optional** on PUT (Decision 13 softened for existing API clients). The console always sends it. Should it become mandatory?
- **Legacy POST /start** is still lifecycle-only (Decision 11). Retire it in Phase 18.
